# 0.1.3c — `Vec` move-only by construction — PLANNED

> **Scope:** the author's answer to the workbench's question 9 (2026-09-25
> 15:45) — **`Vec` becomes MOVE-ONLY BY CONSTRUCTION** — ported from
> `nitpick-regex`, whose design is landed and verified: its 0.0.4d (the marker
> field that makes a `Vec` move-only) **and** its 0.0.4e (`vec_get<T: Pod>`
> through a library `Pod` trait, and a `move` in `vec_pop`, which this
> repository's `vec_pop` already spells). With it, four things the cycle has
> carried to this subcycle: **`vec_at`'s destructive read at an owning `T`**,
> which 0.1.4c measured unreached by the compiler's DEF-104 (`0.1.4c.md` §1.3,
> §11) and which a `Pod` bound turns into the compiler's refusal; **`Bytes.body`
> hidden**, which question 9's answer let ride where the planner judged it
> cheaper — it is, and §1.8 says why; **TM-150's churn pair**, committed at last
> as programs with `heap:` bounds (`0.1.4b.md` §15); and **A′ as a numbered
> decision replacing `VERIFICATION.md` P-1**, which strikes Q-6. And a
> **reason-sweep** (§6.2): every site that cites one of the six defects the
> workbench registry struck at compiler `c970483` as the REASON for a live
> rule, check, test or comment, and what each becomes. **And, taken at planning
> on the orchestrator's instruction**, a numbered decision on how `Pod` relates
> to the prelude `Copy` the author ratified during this planning (the
> compiler's D-327, in no pin of ours): `Pod` now, and a mechanical replacement
> at the re-pin that carries `Copy` (§1.10, PD-49).
>
> **MEASURED FIRST** (§1), at compiler `c970483`, and at `c3bdae2` wherever a
> line says so: the unchanged tree is `GREEN -- 101 unit(s)` (0.1.4c's, verified
> PASS). **The port takes it to `GREEN -- 112`**: seven probes and four unit
> tests join the suite, nothing leaves it, and one file of the defect corpus
> moves from a run to a refusal — `generic_owning_copy/case5`, which exited 11
> on the destructive read and is refused `NITPICK-TYPE-017` at the call (§1.2
> has every `+` and `-`). **Nothing else moves**: the marker alone changes no
> file's verdict in a sweep of all 116; no module's arm bill moves; a `Vec` is

>
> **And two things found at planning.** (1) **The marker forces `vec_at`'s
> shape.** A `Vec` owns once it carries the marker, and at `c970483` the address
> of a LENT owner is `NITPICK-TYPE-085` (the compiler's DEF-102) — so a

> only on loan: a by-value parameter, a field of a lent struct, another
> module's `sealed` field (§1.5). `vec_at` takes a loan, as `nitpick-regex`'s
> `vec_get` does, and every call moves from `@v` to `v`. (2) **One live rule

> `SAFETY.md` §1's move-only row says the language *"does not stop the move
> that faults"* — O-N20's hazard, in a row 0.1.4c's sweep did not reach because
> it cites no id. The reason-sweep found it by shape (§6.2), and §5 corrects it.
>
> **Written 2026-09-26 by the stream-2 planner** (`s2-ntime-0.1.3c-0710`),
> against the tree at `3408c2b` (0.1.4c's record), **and REHEARSED from its own

> commit afterwards; §12 says which command ran where. **The worker re-derives every

> **What comes next** is 0.1.5, the close — the ecosystem's third, which

> it.

**Gate for starting** — every one checked, not assumed:

1. `$WB/BOARD.md` shows `nitpick-time` `CLAIMED s2` and the toolchain
   `c970483`, and `( cd "$WB/.internal/toolchain/c970483" && sha256sum -c
   SHA256SUMS )` prints two `OK`s. **The anchor is unchanged** — `npkrt.o` is
   72 576 bytes, `162b8975…`, at both kept pins (step 1 prints all 64 hex of
   each); if it moved, stop.
2. `git -C "$REPO" status --porcelain` is empty, and `HEAD` is this plan's
   commit or a descendant of it that touched nothing under `src/`, `tests/`,
   `harness/`, `.github/`, `meta/specs/`, `meta/DECISIONS.md`,
   `meta/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md`, `meta/roadmap/0.1/README.md`,
   `meta/roadmap/0.4/README.md`, `meta/roadmap/0.8/README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` or
   `nitpick.toml`. If one did, stop: every `old` text in §4 and §5 was
   measured on this plan's tree.
3. **The decisions are the next six TM numbers.** `grep -o '^### TM-[0-9]*'
   "$REPO/meta/DECISIONS.md" | tail -1` reads `### TM-192` at planning, so
   PD-44 becomes the number after it and PD-45 … PD-49 follow in order. If it
   reads higher, number from there and say so in the record. **This file never
   writes the six numbers out**: `check_refs` reads a TM number in a roadmap
   file as a citation, and `check_specs_current` reads one inside a fence too.
4. **The order is the orchestrator's: 0.1.4c, then 0.1.3c, then 0.1.5.**
   0.1.4c is DONE and verified PASS; if 0.1.5 has started, stop.
5. **Currency: nothing to check.** Every fact here is the compiler's, read in
   its tree at the pin or measured there, or `nitpick-regex`'s, read in its
   tree — never researched on the web (the research skill's §9) — and
   `../../research/CURRENCY.md`'s rows are unchanged.
6. **`earlyoom` is live** (the workbench board's hazard 12): read
   `journalctl -u earlyoom --since today` before the first run and after any
   step that dies by signal (SIGTERM 143, SIGKILL 137). A kill is a stop, never
   a retry. The compiler seat's harnesses may be running beside you: read
   `free -g` before each full run, and run nothing in parallel.
7. **Nothing here waits on the author.** Question 9 and Q-6 are answered.
   **PD-46** (`Bytes.body` hidden) is the planner's judgement under question 9's
   answer, which put it there; it is for the orchestrator, accepted by default,
   and §10 says exactly what to drop if it is struck. **PD-49** records the
   orchestrator's own instruction on `Copy`, and recommends; it changes nothing
   in the tree beyond two comments and one sentence of `SAFETY.md`.

---

## Why `0.1.3c`, and why now

**The letter was given long ago**: the cycle README placed 0.1.3c after 0.1.3b
when question 9 was answered, and 0.1.4, 0.1.4b and 0.1.4c ran first by the
orchestrator's order — 0.1.4 because it holds no `Vec`, 0.1.4b and 0.1.4c
because the loan rules this port meets change at `c970483`, so it is planned
against that pin and not against `c3bdae2`. No number moves.

- *Declined — inside 0.1.5, the close:* a close that changes a type's copy
  semantics and a public signature certifies a surface it has just moved, and
  the audit it triggers would read a close and a design change as one.
- *Declined — two subcycles, the marker and then `Pod`:* `nitpick-regex` needed
  two because the re-pin fell between them; here both land at one pin, and
  `vec_at`'s shape is decided by the marker (§1.5) — split, the first half
  would ship a `vec_at` no lent `Vec` can call.
- *Declined — the churn pair and A′ left to the close:* each is owed to this
  subcycle by name (`0.1.4b.md` §15; Q-6's own note), and each is small.

**The variables.** A Bash call keeps no variables, so they live in a file.
Once, with `REPO` set to the dispatch's absolute `REPO`:

```bash
mkdir -p "$REPO/.internal/w013c"
cat > "$REPO/.internal/w013c/env.sh" <<'EOF2'
export REPO=$(realpath "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../..")
export WB=$(realpath "$REPO/..")
export NPK_TREE=$(realpath "$WB/../nitpick")
export PIN=c970483
export OLD=c3bdae2
export NPKC=$WB/.internal/toolchain/$PIN/npkc
export NPKRT=$WB/.internal/toolchain/$PIN/npkrt.o
export OLD_NPKC=$WB/.internal/toolchain/$OLD/npkc
export OLD_NPKRT=$WB/.internal/toolchain/$OLD/npkrt.o
export W=$REPO/.internal/w013c
export CHECK=$WB/skills/check/scripts
export PLAN=$REPO/meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.3c.md
EOF2
```

— and then **at the top of every shell**, with the dispatch's absolute `REPO`
written out: `source <REPO>/.internal/w013c/env.sh`. `$W` is gitignored scratch
(`.internal`, the harness's `WALK_SKIP`); every script this plan uses lives
there and none is committed — **its text is committed, here**. The harness
reads `NPKC` and `NPKRT` from the environment, so every harness run below is at
`c970483` unless its command sets them otherwise. **Every workbench script runs
under `python3 -B`**, because an import there writes `__pycache__` into a tree
this subcycle may not write; the plan's own scripts do too, so the tree's
`harness/` gains none either.

**The text is applied from this file, not typed from it.** Every block the
worker writes into the tree is a fenced block below headed by a marker line —
`apply GROUP: new PATH` for a new file, `apply GROUP: old PATH` then
`apply GROUP: to PATH` for a replacement, in the groups `code` (§4), `docs`
and `decisions` (§5) — and `script: NAME` for a script; `apply.py` applies one
group in document order, each old text asserted to occur EXACTLY ONCE,
stopping at the first that does not, and `apply.py check GROUP` reports what a
group would do and writes nothing. **A tag is spelled `{{sweep: name=N}}` in
this file** and `apply.py` writes it `[[sweep: name=N]]` into the tree — in an
`old` block as in a `to` block, since both are compared with the tree as
written: a whole tag in a roadmap file is a claim `check_denominators` holds
the tree to the moment the file is saved. **No hunk holds a markdown link** —
`check_refs` resolves links inside this file's fences from THIS file's
directory — so every path in them is plain text in backticks. And **a
decision this subcycle adds is written `PD-44` … `PD-49` outside
`DECISIONS.md` and `TM-<PD-44>` inside it**, which `renumber.py` (step 4)
turns into the numbers. Extract `apply.py` itself, then every script:

```bash
python3 -B - "$PLAN" "$W" <<'EOF2'
import re, sys
text = open(sys.argv[1], encoding="utf-8").read()
m = re.search(r"^<!-- script: apply\.py -->\n(`{3,})[a-z]*\n(.*?)\n\1\n", text, re.S | re.M)
open(sys.argv[2] + "/apply.py", "w", encoding="utf-8").write(m.group(2) + "\n")
print("apply.py:", m.group(2).count("\n") + 1, "lines")
EOF2
cd "$REPO" && python3 -B "$W/apply.py" scripts
```

Expected: `apply.py: 100 lines`, then ten `script` lines in
document order — `apply.py`, `same.py`, `transcript.py` (§4.3), `renumber.py`
(§5.4), `stale.py` and `reasons.py` (§6), `facts.py`, `mut.py`, `sweep.py` and
`ci_read.py` (§7) — and `scripts written: 10`.

<!-- script: apply.py -->
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.3c's text, applied from THIS PLAN rather than transcribed from it
(`0.1.3c.md` §0) -- 0.1.4c's `apply.py`, unchanged but for the plan's name. It
reads and writes every file with `newline=""`, so a byte the plan does not name
is never translated (the workbench PLAYBOOK §6: read source the way the
compiler reads it); and `check GROUP` reports what a group would do and writes
nothing.
Every block the worker applies is a fenced block in the plan headed by one
marker line:

    <!-- apply GROUP: new PATH -->   the block is a new file; PATH must not exist
    <!-- apply GROUP: old PATH -->   the block must occur EXACTLY ONCE in PATH ...
    <!-- apply GROUP: to PATH -->    ... and the next block replaces it
    <!-- script: NAME -->            the block is a script, written to $W/NAME

`python3 apply.py GROUP` applies one group's blocks in document order and
stops at the first that does not apply, having written nothing for it;
`python3 apply.py check GROUP` does the same in memory and writes nothing;
`python3 apply.py scripts` writes every script. So what runs is the plan's
own text, byte for byte."""
import os, re, sys

REPO, W = os.environ["REPO"], os.environ["W"]
PLAN = os.environ.get("PLAN", os.path.join(REPO, "meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.3c.md"))
FENCE = r"\n(?P<fence>`{3,})[a-z]*\n(?P<body>.*?)\n(?P=fence)\n"
EDIT = re.compile(r"^<!-- apply (\w+): (new|old|to) (\S+) -->" + FENCE, re.S | re.M)
SCRIPT = re.compile(r"^<!-- script: (\S+) -->" + FENCE, re.S | re.M)
# A TAG IS SPELLED `{{sweep: name=N}}` IN THE PLAN and written `[[sweep: name=N]]`:
# a whole tag in a roadmap file is a claim `check_denominators` holds the tree to.
TAG = re.compile(r"\{\{(sweep:\s*[a-z_]+\s*=\s*-?\d+)\}\}")


def read(path):
    with open(path, encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        return fh.read()


def write(path, text):
    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        fh.write(text)


text = read(PLAN)
args = sys.argv[1:]
dry = bool(args) and args[0] == "check"
if dry:
    args = args[1:]
if len(args) != 1:
    sys.exit("usage: apply.py [check] GROUP | apply.py scripts")
want = args[0]
if want == "scripts":
    done = 0
    for m in SCRIPT.finditer(text):
        body = m.group("body") + "\n"
        write(os.path.join(W, m.group(1)), body)
        print("  script %-26s %4d lines" % (m.group(1), body.count("\n")))
        done += 1
    print("scripts written: %d" % done)
    sys.exit(0 if done else 1)
pending, done, memory = None, 0, {}
for m in EDIT.finditer(text):
    grp, kind, path = m.group(1), m.group(2), m.group(3)
    body = TAG.sub(r"[[\1]]", m.group("body") + "\n")
    if grp != want:
        continue
    full = os.path.join(REPO, path)
    if kind == "new":
        if os.path.exists(full) or full in memory:
            sys.exit("STOP: %s already exists; `new` writes only a file that does not" % path)
        if dry:
            memory[full] = body
        else:
            os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(full), exist_ok=True)
            write(full, body)
        print("  new    %-58s %4d lines" % (path, body.count("\n")))
        done += 1
    elif kind == "old":
        if pending is not None:
            sys.exit("STOP: an `old %s` with no `to` after it" % pending[0])
        pending = (path, body)
    else:
        if pending is None or pending[0] != path:
            sys.exit("STOP: a `to %s` with no `old` for the same file before it" % path)
        cur = memory[full] if full in memory else read(full)
        n = cur.count(pending[1])
        if n != 1:
            sys.exit("STOP: in %s the old text occurs %d times, not once:\n%s"
                     % (path, n, pending[1][:300]))
        new = cur.replace(pending[1], body)
        if dry:
            memory[full] = new
        else:
            write(full, new)
        print("  edit   %-58s -%d +%d lines" % (path, pending[1].count("\n"), body.count("\n")))
        pending = None
        done += 1
if pending is not None:
    sys.exit("STOP: an `old %s` with no `to` after it" % pending[0])
print("group %s: %d block(s) %s" % (want, done, "would apply" if dry else "applied"))
sys.exit(0 if done else 1)
```

`same.py` compares a saved output with a block this plan expects, line for
line, with each wall-clock figure (`N.N s`) set aside — 0.1.4c's, pointed at
this file. **A command's whole output is compared, never a reading of it**
(the workbench PLAYBOOK §12: care drops lines; a comparator does not).

<!-- script: same.py -->
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.3c: a saved output against the block this plan expects, line for line.

    same.py NAME FILE

reads the fenced block after the marker line `<!-- expect: NAME -->` in the
plan and the lines of FILE, sets every wall-clock figure (`12.3 s`) aside in
both, and prints each line that differs, both ways, then `NAME: N line(s)
expected, D difference(s)`. Exit 0 only at 0 -- so the comparison is the
script's and not a reading."""
import os, re, sys

REPO = os.environ["REPO"]
PLAN = os.environ.get("PLAN", os.path.join(REPO, "meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.3c.md"))
name, path = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
text = open(PLAN, encoding="utf-8").read()
m = re.search(r"^<!-- expect: %s -->\n(`{3,})[a-z]*\n(.*?)\n\1\n" % re.escape(name),
              text, re.S | re.M)
if not m:
    sys.exit("STOP: the plan has no expected block named %r" % name)
CLOCK = re.compile(r"\b\d+\.\d+ s\b")


def lines(s):
    return [CLOCK.sub("N s", l.rstrip()) for l in s.splitlines()]


want, got = lines(m.group(2)), lines(open(path, encoding="utf-8").read())
diffs = 0
for i in range(max(len(want), len(got))):
    w = want[i] if i < len(want) else "(no line)"
    g = got[i] if i < len(got) else "(no line)"
    if w != g:
        diffs += 1
        print("  line %d\n    plan: %s\n    run:  %s" % (i + 1, w, g))
print("%s: %d line(s) expected, %d difference(s)" % (name, len(want), diffs))
sys.exit(0 if diffs == 0 else 1)
```

**Every removal in this plan names its target through `"${W:?}"`** or
`"${REPO:?}"`, never a bare variable: an unset one would turn `rm -rf "$W/…"`
into a removal at the filesystem's root, and this environment's safety check
refuses the bare form.

## 1. What was measured — the facts this plan rests on

All at `c970483` unless a line says `c3bdae2`; LLVM 20.1.2; every program
through `npkc`, `llc`, `ld.lld` and a run, at -O0 and again through
`opt -O2`, the same on both legs unless a line says otherwise. **Step 1's
`facts.py` re-derives every line of this section before anything is changed**
— it applies this plan's own `code` group to a scratch copy of the tree, so
what it measures is the planned text — and its whole output is §7's `facts`
block. The variants it names: `before` is the tree at this plan's commit;
`port` is `before` with §4 applied; `marker` is `before` with only the marker;
and `marker_int64`, `podptr`, `loan_nobound`, `port_req`, `clone_raw` and
`clone_plain` are `port` with one named change each (§1.3 to §1.9 say which).

Unchanged since 0.1.4c: `c970483`, both digests equal to `SHA256SUMS`, and
`npkrt.o` 72 576 bytes, `162b8975…`, the same object at `c3bdae2` — so every
`heap:` and `cap:` number the harness asserts is the runtime's same
arithmetic at both pins, and §1.7's pair is measured at both.

### 1.2 The unchanged tree, and the count the port reaches: 101 → 112

stage splits them `86 + 28 + 2`. With §4 and §5 applied: **`GREEN -- 112

| probes, `tests/probe/*.npk` | 42 | **49** | **+7**: `probe16f`, `probe16g`, `probe16h` (a `Vec` copied, assigned, and held by a struct copied — `TYPE-046`), `probe16i` (a write through `Bytes.body` — `TYPE-080`), `probe18` (the zero-length owner, run), `probe18b` (its copy — `TYPE-046`), `probe19` (`Pod` for `string` as declared — `TYPE-047`) |
| unit, `tests/unit/*.npk` | 17 | **21** | **+4**: `vec_moves`, `vec_at_pod`, and the churn pair `vec_churn_pop` and `vec_churn_clear` |

### 1.3 The marker, measured

The shape is `nitpick-regex`'s, chosen there from six candidates each built and
swept at `c3bdae2` (its `0.0.4d.md` §1.2): the struct's last field is
`hidden string[0]:move_only`, filled `[]` by the one constructor, `vec_init`.
**Why it works**, as `nitpick-regex` read it in the compiler and this tree
re-measures: the compiler answers whether an ARRAY owns by its element,
whatever its length, and marks a struct owning when any field is (D-183); an
owner is move-only (`TYPE-046`); the array's size is its element's times zero;
and `[]` is the zero array (D-139). The compiler's `TYPE_REFERENCE.md` §9.2
states `T[0]` supported since its 1.6.0 step 3g.

Measured here, at both pins:

- **the marker alone moves no file**: every one of the 116 tracked `.npk`
  compiled against `marker`'s `src/` gives the verdict it gives against
  `before`'s — no copy of a `Vec` exists in the tree, and no function in `src/`
  takes one by value (`facts` part D);
- **it costs nothing**: `#size_of<Vec<int64>>()` and `#size_of<Vec<string>>()`
  are 24 and `#size_of<Bytes>()` 32, before and after (E1); the arm bill
  `NITPICK-REACH-002` asks of a consumer of `vec.npk`, of `bytes.npk` and of
  the umbrella is 9, 12 and 13 identities, the same before and after (part F);
- **the block is still `wild`**: a `Vec` never freed exits 96, `WildLeak`, on
  the `port` exactly as on `before` (E6) — the drop the compiler generates for
  a `Vec` now walks the empty array and frees nothing, so `vec_free` is still
  the block's one release, and every test that calls it still passes;
- **each copy shape is refused, once, at the copy** (E2 … E4): `Vec<int64>:w =
  v;`, `v = u;`, and a copy of a struct holding a `Vec`, each
  `NITPICK-TYPE-046`, *"`Vec<int64>` owns storage that is released at scope
  exit, so it is move-only (D-183)"*;
- **control A**: against `before`, the same three files compile, link, and
  read the allocator's free poison through their second handle — each pops
  from a block freed through the first — exit 70 on both legs;
- **control B**: against `marker_int64` — the same field over `int64[0]`, an
  element that owns nothing — they compile and exit 70 again. The element's
  ownership is the mechanism, not the zero length.

### 1.4 What move-only refuses, and what it still allows

| Written | At `c970483` with the marker |
|---|---|
| `Vec<int64>:w = v;` / `v = u;` / a struct holding a `Vec`, copied | **`NITPICK-TYPE-046`**, once each — `probe16f`, `probe16g`, `probe16h` |
| `Vec<int64>:w = move(v);`, then read through `w` | runs — `vec_moves` |
| a struct built by moving a `Vec` in, read through a loan of the struct, then moved | runs — `vec_moves` |
| a `move Vec<int64>:v` parameter, called `consume(move(k.v))`, freeing inside | runs, the block freed once — `vec_moves` |
| an element-by-element copy into a fresh `Vec`, the original then moved away and freed | runs; the copy keeps its elements — `vec_moves` |
| `vec_free(@v)` after `move(v)` | **`NITPICK-MOVE-001`** at both pins — as before the marker, a moved binding is dead |
| `move(v)` of an ordinary by-value `Vec` parameter | **`NITPICK-TYPE-047`**, *"this parameter was lent, not given"* (it compiled before the marker: a non-owning `Vec` moved was its copy) |

**The language fact under it is pinned with no library code**: `probe18` — a
struct whose one owning field is `string[0]` is 24 bytes, is built, moved and
dropped a thousand times without a fault, and its `int64[0]` twin copies freely
— and `probe18b`, the copy of that struct, `NITPICK-TYPE-046`. If a later
compiler refuses `T[0]`, sizes it, or stops counting its element, these two
redden before the three library fixtures do, and name the cause.
`nitpick-regex`'s fallback, measured there, is `hidden string:move_only` holding
`""`: the same verdicts, 24 bytes more on every `Vec`.

### 1.5 The loan: why `vec_at` reads one

**Found at planning, and it decides `vec_at`'s signature.** An ordinary
parameter is a loan (D-065, D-183), and at `c970483` a loan of an OWNER is
read-only: every write-capable access through it, `@v` included, is
`NITPICK-TYPE-085` (the compiler's DEF-102). Before the marker a `Vec` owned
nothing, so a function handed one by value could take `@v` and call any
`vec_*` function on its own copy of the header; after it, it cannot. Measured,
with `vec_at` bounded but still taking `Vec<T>->` (the `podptr` variant) and
with it taking a loan (`port`), each reading element 0 (E7):

| The `Vec` the reader holds | `vec_at(Vec<T>->:v, …)` — `podptr` | `vec_at(Vec<T>:v, …)` — `port` |
|---|---|---|
| a by-value parameter | `vec_at::<int64>(@v, 0i64)`: **`NITPICK-TYPE-085`** | `vec_at::<int64>(v, 0i64)`: runs |
| a field of a lent struct | `@h.v`: **`NITPICK-TYPE-085`** | `h.v`: runs |
| another module's `sealed` `Vec` field | `@b.items`: **`NITPICK-TYPE-079`** (D-313 counts `@` as a write) | `b.items`: runs |
| a pointer's pointee | `p`: runs | `<-p`: runs |

So a pointer `vec_at` serves only a caller that owns the `Vec` locally or holds
a pointer to it; every other reader — a formatter handed a `Layout` by value at
cycle 0.4, a consumer reading a struct's `sealed` `Vec` — could not read an
element at all. As a loan it reads every one of them, and nothing it does can
write the caller's block: the compiler refuses that now, where the pointer
signature left it to `vec_at`'s restraint. It is `nitpick-regex`'s `vec_get`
shape, whose reasons its RX-162 gives and all three of which hold here. The
turbofish in these programs is deliberate: without it, a refused `@v` at a
generic call adds `NITPICK-TYPE-022` (*"`T` cannot be inferred"*), the
compiler's DEF-117 cascade, fixed at its next landing and not in this pin.
**Every call in the tree moves from `@v` to `v`**: thirteen — ten in
`vec_boundaries`, one in `vec_at_past_end`, two in `case5`.

### 1.6 `Pod`, `case5`, and the one hole

`pub trait:Pod = { func:pod_copy = Self(Self:self) never fails; };` and its
nine impls — `int8` … `int64`, `uint8` … `uint64`, `bool`, each `pass self` of a
LENT `self` — are `nitpick-regex`'s, text for text and mode for mode (its
`src/core/vec.npk`, RX-168), declared in `vec.npk` and re-exported by the
umbrella. `vec_at<T: Pod> = T(Vec<T>:v, int64:i)` keeps its slice over `count`
(TM-129) and ends `pass raw s[i].pod_copy();`. Measured:

- **`vec.npk` and the umbrella compile in every variant at both pins**, `port`
  included (part C) — the design is not the pin's;
- **an owning type cannot implement `Pod` as declared** (E9): `impl:string:Pod`,
  an impl for a struct holding a `string`, and `impl:Vec<int64>:Pod`, each
  written `pass self`, are each `NITPICK-TYPE-047`. `probe19` asserts the
  first. A consumer's struct of two integers, and a four-variant payload enum
  of `FORMAT_MODEL.md`'s kind, each implement it in one line through the
  umbrella and read back TWICE at one index (`vec_at_pod`, in the full run);
- **the guard is unchanged**: a read at `count` and a negative read still trap
  `OutOfBounds`, 94 (E11; `vec_at_past_end` in the full run);
- **`case5`** — the library's own `vec_at` at `T = string` — in three worlds at
  both pins (E8): against `before`, the file as it stands exits **11**, the
  destructive read, `pass` of a place moving the element out and leaving
  `count` untouched; its new text against `loan_nobound` — `vec_at` taking its
  loan with NO bound — exits **11** as well: through a `#wild_slice` over the
  lent header, the loan alone still moves the element out of the caller's
  block, the refusal routed around; and against `port`, **`NITPICK-TYPE-017`**
  at both calls, *"`string` does not implement `Pod`, which `vec_at`'s parameter
  `T` requires"*. So the bound closes it, and the loan does not.
  **What `case5` becomes** (PD-45): it stays where it is, in
  `generic_owning_copy/`, and asserts the refusal — its marker moves to
  `expect-error: NITPICK-TYPE-017`, its two calls read the `Vec` as a loan (with
  `@v` they add `NITPICK-TYPE-007` to the refusal, a pointer where a `Vec` is
  lent), its header keeps its 0.0.5 record under the new paragraph, and its
  `TRANSCRIPT.txt` gains the three rows above (§4.3);
- **the alternative the language offers is refused** (part C): `.clone()` under
  the prelude's `Clone` — whose `clone` MAY FAIL — is `NITPICK-TYPE-042` under
  `raw` (`clone_raw`) and `NITPICK-TYPE-007` without it (`clone_plain`), an
  error channel on every read.

**The one hole, a compiler defect already raised** (E10): an impl declaring
`move string:self` where `Pod` lends it is ACCEPTED at `c970483` — the
compiler's DEF-116, raised by `nitpick-regex`'s 0.0.4e planning and fixed at
the compiler's notice 69, which is not in this pin — and through `vec_at` it
hands back a second owner of the element: read twice, one body freed twice,
**95**, on both legs at both pins. `TYPE-047`'s own message suggests exactly
that `move`, so `vec.npk`'s comment and `SAFETY.md` S-18h say not to take it.
**No impl in this library does it**, the port's impls are exact, and the
compiler seat measured `nitpick-regex`'s `probe18_impl_adds_move.npk` as the
ecosystem's whole exposure to the fix — so this repository adds no second pin
of the shape (PD-45 records why).

### 1.7 The churn pair — derived from the source, then measured

TM-150 measured it at cycle 0.1.0b in a scratch directory whose programs are
lost; the numbers were quoted in `SAFETY.md` S-18d, `src/core/vec.npk` and the
workbench's playbook with no program behind them. The committed pair imports the
REAL `src/core/vec.npk`, so it guards the library's own `vec_pop` and
`vec_clear`:

| | `vec_churn_pop.npk` — the remedy | `vec_churn_clear.npk` — the leak |
|---|---|---|
| each of 2 000 000 cycles | `vec_push` a 24-byte string, then `vec_pop` it into a binding that dies with the iteration | `vec_push` the same, then `vec_clear` |
| derived from the source | block 4 × 24 = 96 bytes, never grown; one 24-byte body live at a time: `peak_live` 96 + 24 = **120**; `count` 1 + 2 000 000 | the block, and every body orphaned: `peak_live` 96 + 2 000 000 × 24 = **48 000 096**; `count` the same |
| measured, both pins, both legs (E13) | `allocated=48000096 peak_live=120 count=2000001` | `allocated=48000096 peak_live=48000096 count=2000001` |
| under `ulimit -v 65536` (E14) | exit 0 | exit **92**, `HeapOom` |
| its bounds | `heap: count >= 2000000`, `heap: peak_live <= 75000`, `cap: 65536 KiB, exit 0` | `heap: peak_live >= 48000000`, `cap: 65536 KiB, exit 92` |

Exactly TM-150's numbers. The remedy's ceiling is TM-185's rule — the geometric
mean of 120 and 48 000 096, 75 894, rounded down to two significant figures —
and its work floor is the allocations its loop must make; the leak's floor is
its leak's arithmetic, the bodies alone. **A cap goes on it** because
`TESTING.md` V-17 puts one on a pair with opposite outcomes under it, and this
pair has them. Every bound is seen red on its mutant in §7 (`mut.py`, rows K1 …
K4), and the declined "ceiling alone" is fooled by an emptied loop (D1).

### 1.8 `Bytes.body` hidden — cheaper here, and the count is sixteen

The author's answer to question 9: *"The `Bytes.buf` accessor rides with it
where its planner judges that cheaper, and says so."* **Judged cheaper here, and
said so (PD-46).** A `sealed` field admits a write THROUGH its pointer (D-313):
measured by cycle 0.1.0c at `c3bdae2`, a consumer's `b.body.ptr[0i64] = 65u8`
compiled and ran, `bytes_view` then read 65 where the sink wrote 97, and a
write one byte past the allocation ran at exit 0 — memory a consumer can
corrupt through the public surface, with no diagnostic. `hidden` refuses the
member access itself (D-314): the same write is **`NITPICK-TYPE-080`**, once
(E5; `probe16i`), and against `before` the fixture compiles and reads the
consumer's byte, **exit 70**. **The cost, counted two ways that agree** — the
code lines outside `bytes.npk` naming `body`, and the compiler's own
`TYPE-080`s when it is hidden and the tests are not yet moved, each file's one
`note` aside (E15) — is **sixteen lines in three test files**:
`bytes_growth` 11, `bytes_view_lifetime` 4, `probe16e` 1, each of which reads
`raw bytes_capacity(@b)` now. Nothing in `src/` outside `bytes.npk` reads
`body`; the two heap-bounded `Bytes` tests measure what they did, 2 048 636
and 160; no arm bill moves (part F: `bytes.npk` 12 before and after).
`bytes_capacity` takes `Bytes->`, as `bytes_len` does. **Why now and not
later**: cycle 0.4's formatters are `Bytes`' consumers, and hiding the field
after they are written against it costs their call sites too; `nitpick-regex`
took the same change beside its marker (its RX-163).

### 1.9 A′, and what a live `requires` would cost

Q-6 is answered — A′, 2026-09-25 15:56 — and its numbered decision is owed here
(Q-6's own note). **Nothing in `src/` changes under it**: the obligations were
comments already (TM-164), so the edits are the sentences that cite P-1 or say
"until Q-6 is answered". Rule P-1b carries one clause of `nitpick-regex`'s that
this repository had not measured — *never a `requires` on an accessor whose
body already stops* — so it is measured here, on `port_req`, `port` with
`requires i >= 0i64 && i < v.count` live on `vec_at` (E11, E12): a read past
`count` stops at **116**, `RequiresViolated`, where the slice stops it at
**94**, `OutOfBounds`, at both pins; and a consumer of `vec.npk` owes **ten**
identities where it owed nine, the tenth `RequiresViolated`. The contract would
pre-empt the guard, change the stop's identity, and charge every consumer an
arm, for a check the guard already makes.

### 1.10 The prelude's `Copy`, ratified during this planning — and why `Pod` anyway (PD-49)

**Found at planning, from the orchestrator's supplement of 2026-09-26**: the
author ratified the copy marker the compiler seat had put to him, as the
compiler's **D-327** — the number is the compiler seat's notice 70, relayed by
the orchestrator; the marker's row in the compiler's tree at `9f6f370`
(`meta/roadmap/OPEN_DECISIONS.md`) is still the open suggestion, recording the
relayed approval and saying the number waits for the author's word in the
compiler session. D-327 is a PRELUDE marker trait `Copy`, with impls for every
copyable scalar in the generated scalar region, a derivable form for a struct of
copyables, and `List<T: Copy>`'s `list_get` returning `T` `never fails`; no
`never fails` clone for an owning type. It lands at the compiler's 1.6.1c,
after an advance notice, and `Copy` becomes a prelude-owned name (D-239). **It
is in no pin of ours**, and no spelling of it can be measured here. The two
courses, and what each costs:

| | **`Pod` now, `Copy` mechanically at its re-pin — CHOSEN** | the `T: Pod` bound deferred until `Copy` lands |
|---|---|---|
| `vec_at` at an owning `T` | refused, `NITPICK-TYPE-017`, from this subcycle | `case5`'s destructive read stays public behaviour for three landings and a re-pin |
| `vec_at`'s shape meanwhile | a loan, bounded — the only shape that reads a lent `Vec` safely (§1.5) | no good interim: a pointer reader cannot read a lent `Vec` once the marker lands, and a loan WITHOUT the bound moves an owning element out of the caller's block (`case5` against `loan_nobound`, 11) — so the marker would have to wait too, and question 9's answer with it |
| what the later re-pin does | deletes `Pod`'s block and its umbrella line, rebounds one function, respells one call, and puts three test impls in `Copy`'s form — measured then | writes the bound, the loan and the marker at once, against a pin that also changes the view rules |
| cost | thirteen lines of trait and impls, and one public name taken away later — which TM-013 allows while the library is `0.x` | the holes above, for the whole interval |

**So `Pod` is ported now, and SHAPED for the replacement**: it is one block of
`vec.npk` (the trait and its nine impls, contiguous), one bound (`vec_at`'s),
one call (`vec_at`'s `pod_copy()`), one umbrella line, and three impls in
`tests/` — `vec_at_pod`'s two and `probe19`'s — and it is used nowhere else;
nothing in this library is named `Copy`, which would collide with the
prelude-owned name at that re-pin. `nitpick-regex`'s `vec_get` has the same
shape, so the same replacement is its adoption's too (§11). *Also declined*:
naming our trait `Copy` now, so the replacement is a deletion (it collides at
the re-pin, and the tree would go red for a reason that is not the re-pin's);
and holding 0.1.3c until `Copy` lands (the orchestrator's order is 0.1.3c, then
the close).

### 1.11 What else was checked, so a worker does not wonder

- **The umbrella re-exports a trait**: `pub use "./core/vec.npk".Pod;` compiles,
  and a consumer that imports `src/lib.npk` alone implements `Pod` for its own
  types (`vec_at_pod`). The umbrella goes from 57 to 59 names with
  `bytes_capacity`.
- **`vec_pop` is untouched**: it already spells `move` through its local slice,
  which 0.1.4c measured the DEF-104 gate admits; its comment gains a paragraph
  pointing at the churn pair.
- **The loan changes no heap number**: the six files 0.1.4b bounded measure what
  they did, unit for unit (the full run, §7 step 6).
- **The DEF-107 view freeze is not in this pin** (the compiler's notice 67), and
  its rule was read, not built: a view's root is frozen for the view's
  lifetime against every WRITE-capable access, and a callee that writes nothing
  freezes nothing. The only new call made while a view is live is
  `bytes_view_lifetime`'s `raw bytes_capacity(@b)`, whose body reads one field
  and writes none; `vec_at`'s own slice is a view of what `v.items` reaches and
  nothing in `vec_at` writes. The compiler seat measured its exposure over the
  231 files it swept as zero; the eleven files this subcycle adds were not
  among them, and §11 hands their re-measurement to the next re-pin.
- **The harness is not touched but for counts and comments**: no check, no
  stage and no self-check plant changes; `check_raw_index`'s `.ptr[` half
  becomes a belt behind the compiler, as its `.items[` half did at 0.1.0c.
- **`tests/rejection/` stays empty**: this library's refusals have lived in
  `tests/probe/` since cycle 0.1.0c's `probe16` family, dispatched by their own
  headers (B-4c), and the four new library refusals extend that family.
- **`CURRENCY.md`** — unchanged; nothing here depends on the world outside the
  compiler tree and `nitpick-regex`'s.

## 2. The decisions, and what chose each

Six, drafted here and recorded by the worker in `../../DECISIONS.md` as the
next six TM numbers (§5.3 is their full text, alternatives included; §10
lists them).

- **PD-44 — `Vec<T>` is move-only by construction.** The last field is
  `hidden string[0]:move_only`, filled `[]` by `vec_init`; a copy of a `Vec`,
  one `Vec` assigned over another, and a copy of a struct holding one are each
  `NITPICK-TYPE-046` (§1.3, §1.4). Replaces TM-156 in part — its "What it does
  NOT do", for `Vec` — and adds `SAFETY.md` S-18g. *Declined:*
  `nitpick-regex`'s other five candidate markers, each measured there (a
  `string`, a `buffer`, a `string?`, an `OwnedFd`, the block in a managed
  `buffer`), the prelude's `List<T>`, a harness check refusing a copy in
  `src/`, and accept-and-document — the author declined the last on question 9.
- **PD-45 — `vec_at` takes `T: Pod` and reads through a loan; `case5` asserts
  the refusal.** `Pod` and its nine impls are `nitpick-regex`'s, exact;
  `vec_at<T: Pod> = T(Vec<T>:v, int64:i)` over the same slice, `pass raw
  s[i].pod_copy();`; the umbrella re-exports `Pod`. Replaces TM-136 in part —
  "`vec_at<T>` is destructive" — and adds `SAFETY.md` S-18h. **The loan is
  the marker's consequence (§1.5)**, not a taste. *Declined:* `vec_at` as it
  stands (it compiles, and removes the element); the bound with `Vec<T>->`
  kept (a lent `Vec` unreadable); the loan without the bound (`case5` exits 11
  through it); `.clone()` under `Clone` (fallible, `TYPE-042`/`TYPE-007`);
  `struct:Vec<T: Pod>` (refuses the churn pair and every owning measurement,
  and waits on DEF-116, since a `move`-adding impl would satisfy it —
  `nitpick-regex` holds it open, and this library follows that answer);
  renaming `vec_at` to `vec_get`; `Pod` imported from `nitpick-regex` (TM-027);
  the name `Copy`; a second pin of DEF-116's shape (the compiler seat measured
  `nitpick-regex`'s as the whole exposure). **What `case5` becomes**: a
  refusal in place — *declined*: moving it to `tests/probe/` (it is the
  corpus's record of the destructive read, and its transcript is there);
  deleting it (a reproduction is never deleted); keeping its `exit 11` against
  an unexported copy of the old `vec_at` (a test of a function nothing ships).
- **PD-46 — `Bytes.body` is `hidden`; `bytes_capacity` reads the capacity.**
  Question 9's answer left it to the planner where cheaper, and §1.8 measures
  why it is: sixteen test lines, one accessor, one re-export, one refusal
  probe. Replaces TM-156 in part — `body` among the sealed fields, and its
  declined "`body` hidden now". *Declined:* sealed as it stands (the bytes
  writable through the public surface); its own subcycle after the close (the
  same lines later, after 0.4's formatters are written against the field); a
  loan-taking accessor (every other `Bytes` reader takes `Bytes->`).
- **PD-47 — TM-150's churn pair is committed**, `vec_churn_pop.npk` and
  `vec_churn_clear.npk` in `tests/unit/`, with TM-185's bounds and TM-186's
  cap (§1.7). *Declined:* one program for both (a whole-run high-water mark
  would hide the pop's 120); `tests/probe/` (the pair asserts the library's own
  verbs); no cap (V-17's rule admits one, and every twin pair then carries both
  instruments).
- **PD-48 — A′ replaces `VERIFICATION.md` P-1 as rule P-1b; Q-6 is struck.**
  A live contract only where a numbered decision accepts its arm — today
  TM-156's `ListLen`; `prove` a comment until the verified build; never a
  `requires` on caller input or on an accessor whose body already stops
  (§1.9 measures the last). *Declined:* Q-6's A, B and C, each for the reason
  Q-6 recorded.
- **PD-49 — `Pod` now, and the prelude's `Copy` (the compiler's D-327)
  replaces it mechanically at the re-pin that carries it**, in this library and
  in `nitpick-regex`; nothing here is named `Copy` (§1.10). *Declined:*
  deferring the bound until `Copy` lands (`case5`'s read stays public, and the
  marker must wait with it); naming the trait `Copy` now (it collides with the
  prelude-owned name); holding the subcycle.

## 3. The commits, and why CI never judges a half-applied tree

**Two commits from the worker; this plan's is before them.**

| | Commit | Tree state | Local harness | Pushed |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| plan | `cycle 0.1: the plan for 0.1.3c, …` — this file, the cycle README's row and checklist, `ROADMAP.md`'s row | unported; CI at `c970483` | `GREEN -- 101` (no code changed) | **by the orchestrator, after it verifies the plan** — never by the planner |
| **1** | `cycle 0.1.3c: Vec move-only by construction — …` — everything in §4 and §5 | ported | **`GREEN -- 112`** | yes |
| **2** | `cycle 0.1.3c: the record — CI run <id> at c970483` — the execution record, the REPORT, the cycle README's boxes, `ROADMAP.md`'s row | | unchanged | yes |

**One work commit, because the port has no coherent half**: the marker
without the loan leaves no lent `Vec` readable (§1.5); the loan without its
call sites is thirteen refusals; `Bytes.body` hidden without its sixteen reads
is sixteen more; and the documents cite the decisions. CI's pin does not move
(`c970483`), so no line of `ci.yml` but its prose comment changes.
**Commit 2 exists because a CI result cannot live inside the commit that
caused it** — 0.1.4c's shape.

**If commit 1's CI run is RED:** read the job's own log through
`gh api repos/alternative-intelligence-cp/nitpick-time/actions/jobs/<job>/logs`
(`gh run view --log` can print nothing), never the summary (TM-146). A red the
local harness reproduces is a defect in commit 1: fix it in a new commit and
push. A red it does not reproduce is an environment difference and a finding:
record it, fix forward, push. **Never re-run to get a green.**

**The push is the worker's** unless the dispatch's `NOTES:` says the
orchestrator pushes: `git -C "$REPO" push`, and nothing else — no force, no
branch.

---

Group `code`, applied at step 2: `src/`, the tests, the harness's counts and
comments, the manifest's counts, and CI's prose. **The transcript section is
not a block**: §4.3's script generates it at step 3 from the files this group
writes. §4.1 to §4.7 are in the order `apply.py` applies them.

section's sample; the header's last section, whose present tense about a

refuses; the struct and its comment; `vec_init`'s literal; `Pod` and `vec_at`;
a paragraph above `vec_pop`; and one above `vec_free`. The function bodies that
change are three lines: the struct, the literal, and `vec_at`'s signature and

says how the prelude's `Copy` replaces it (PD-49).

```
// rather than opaque. That build is cycle 0.8's (`VERIFICATION.md` P-1b);
// nothing here claims it yet.
//
// WHAT HIDING AND SEALING DID NOT STOP, UNTIL CYCLE 0.1.3c: A WHOLE-STRUCT
// COPY. A `Vec<T>` was a plain struct over a `wild` pointer, so
// `Vec<int64>:w = v;` compiled and made a SECOND HANDLE on the same block, and
// `v = u;` overwrote all three fields at once from outside this module --
// neither names a field, so neither is a write the seal sees. Measured at
// `c3bdae2`: after the copy, `vec_free(@v)` and then `vec_at(@w, 0i64)` read
// the allocator's `0xAA` poison (exit 170) -- a use-after-free with no hidden
// or sealed field ever named. The workbench's question 9 carried it to the
// author beside `Bytes`' pointee, and the next section is the answer.
//
// ===========================================================================
// MOVE-ONLY BY CONSTRUCTION (cycle 0.1.3c, PD-44; `SAFETY.md` S-18g)
// ===========================================================================
//
// THE AUTHOR'S ANSWER TO QUESTION 9 (2026-09-25), AND `nitpick-regex`'S
// DESIGN, PORTED (its RX-161). The struct's last field is
// `hidden string[0]:move_only`, and each shape above is `NITPICK-TYPE-046`
// where it is written: a copy of a `Vec`, one `Vec` assigned over another, and
// a copy of a struct holding one (`../../tests/probe/probe16f_vec_copy_refused.npk`,
// `probe16g_vec_assign_refused.npk` and `probe16h_vec_holder_copy_refused.npk`;
// against the tree before, each compiles and reads the free poison). A transfer
// is `move(v)`, after which `v` is refused (`NITPICK-MOVE-001`), and a
// `move Vec<T>:v` parameter takes one; `../../tests/unit/vec_moves.npk` runs
// every shape the property still allows. It costs nothing measured: a `Vec`
// is 24 bytes, as before; no module's arm bill moves; and the drop the
// compiler now generates for a `Vec` walks the empty array and frees NOTHING,
// so the block is still `wild`, `vec_free` is still its release, and a `Vec`
// never freed still traps `WildLeak` at `exit 0` (96, measured).
//
// A BY-VALUE `Vec` PARAMETER IS A LOAN, AND A LOAN OF AN OWNER IS READ-ONLY.
// An ordinary parameter is one the callee may read and may not keep (the
// compiler's D-065 and D-183), so a `Vec` is passed to one without `move`, and
// every write through it -- `@v`, an assignment into it -- is
// `NITPICK-TYPE-085` (the compiler's DEF-102). That is why `vec_at`, the one
// function here that only reads, takes its `Vec` as a loan, and every function
// that changes one takes `Vec<T>->`.
mod:vec;
```

```
// function reaches the struct through a pointer and assigns the field, which is
// a write point, not an address.
pub struct:Vec<T> = { hidden wild T->:items; sealed limit<ListLen> int64:count; sealed limit<ListLen> int64:cap; };

// `ensures answer.count == 0 && answer.cap >= 1`
//
// (`VERIFICATION.md` P-1: contracts are written as comments in the syntax they
// will take at 1.5. `answer` and not `result` -- `result` is a reserved
// `VerificationKeyword`, TM-130/B-18.)
```
<!-- apply code: to src/core/vec.npk -->
```
// function reaches the struct through a pointer and assigns the field, which is
// a write point, not an address.
//
// `move_only`, THE FOURTH FIELD (cycle 0.1.3c, PD-44): a fixed array of NO
// strings. It holds nothing and occupies nothing -- `#size_of<Vec<int64>>()`
// is 24, as it was -- and because its ELEMENT owns, the compiler's layout walk
// marks every `Vec<T>` as owning (its D-183), and an owning type is move-only.
// That is the whole mechanism; the header's last section says what it refuses.
// The same field over `int64[0]`, whose element owns nothing, changes nothing
// at all (measured): the element's ownership is what does it. `hidden`, so no
// module but this one can name it, and `vec_init` fills it with `[]`.
pub struct:Vec<T> = { hidden wild T->:items; sealed limit<ListLen> int64:count; sealed limit<ListLen> int64:cap; hidden string[0]:move_only; };

// `ensures answer.count == 0 && answer.cap >= 1`
//
// (`VERIFICATION.md` P-1b: an obligation is a comment in the syntax it would
// take unless a numbered decision accepts the arm its live clause costs every
// consumer. `answer` and not `result` -- `result` is a reserved
// `VerificationKeyword`, TM-130/B-18.)
```

```
// `requires i >= 0 && i < v.count`
//
// RETURNS BY VALUE, SO THIS IS FOR A SCALAR `T` -- and the reason is not the
// one written here until 0.0.5, which was wrong in both halves. It said "at an
// owning `T` a copy is refused by TYPE-046". Nothing refuses it, and what
// happens instead is not a copy: `pass` of a PLACE at an owning type MOVES
// IMPLICITLY, so at `T = string` this function REMOVES the element from the
// `Vec` -- the emitted body stores `zeroinitializer` over the slot and calls
// `npk.vacant.<dty>` on it -- while leaving `count` untouched, so the
// container still claims to hold it. Measured: a second `vec_at` of the same
// index returns a length-0 string
// (`../../tests/probe/defect/generic_owning_copy/case5_vec_at_destructive.npk`,
// exit 11). That is the LANGUAGE behaving as specified and is not a defect;
// it is a reason this row is for a non-owning `T` (TM-136).
//
// A family that wants an owning element reads through `v.items[i]` at the site
// that knows the element's shape. That is the compiler's own note on `List<T>`
// and the reason there is no generic `list_at` there either.
pub func:vec_at<T> = T(Vec<T>->:v, int64:i) never fails {
    T[]:s = #wild_slice<T>(v.items, v.count);
    pass s[i];
};
```
<!-- apply code: to src/core/vec.npk -->
```
// ---- `Pod`: what `vec_at` may hand back by value (PD-45; `SAFETY.md` S-18h) --
//
// ONE METHOD, AND THE LANGUAGE DECIDES WHO MAY IMPLEMENT IT. `pod_copy` takes
// its `self` LENT and returns a `Self`, never failing. For a type that owns
// nothing, `pass self` is its copy. For an owning type the same body is
// `NITPICK-TYPE-047` -- a lent owner cannot be passed on (the compiler's
// D-065) -- so an impl the compiler admits for one would have to build an
// independent value, and a `never fails` body cannot allocate one. Measured at
// compiler `c970483`: `impl:string:Pod`, an impl for a struct holding a
// `string`, and `impl:Vec<int64>:Pod`, each written `pass self`, are each
// `TYPE-047` (`../../tests/probe/probe19_pod_owner_refused.npk` asserts the
// first). So `T: Pod` on `vec_at` states TM-132's restriction TO THE COMPILER
// for the one verb that hands an element back, at every consumer's call.
//
// THE TRAIT AND ITS NINE IMPLS ARE `nitpick-regex`'s, TEXT FOR TEXT, THE
// PARAMETER MODES INCLUDED (its RX-168); this library imports nothing
// (TM-027), so the trait is declared here rather than shared. The integers to
// 64 bits and `bool`: every scalar a specification here stores in a `Vec`. A
// type of a consumer's that owns nothing implements it in one line where the
// type is declared -- a struct of integers, or a payload enum such as
// `FORMAT_MODEL.md`'s parts -- and the umbrella re-exports the name so a
// consumer can (`../../tests/unit/vec_at_pod.npk`). A wider scalar is one line
// here.
//
// THE HOLE, SAID WHERE A READER MEETS IT: at compiler `c970483` an impl may
// declare its `self` `move` where the trait lends it, and the compiler accepts
// it -- the compiler's DEF-116, raised by `nitpick-regex`'s planning, whose
// fix (a parameter's `move` made part of the signature, refused
// `NITPICK-TYPE-014`) is at no pin of ours. Through `vec_at` such an impl hands
// back a second owner of the element: `impl:string:Pod` written with
// `move string:self`, read twice, frees one body twice, exit 95 on both legs
// (measured, `meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.3c.md` §1.6). `TYPE-047`'s own message
// suggests exactly that `move` -- DO NOT TAKE IT HERE. No impl in this library
// does, and `nitpick-regex`'s `probe18_impl_adds_move.npk` pins the shape for
// the ecosystem.
//
// THE PRELUDE'S `Copy` WILL REPLACE THIS TRAIT (PD-49). On 2026-09-26 the
// author ratified the copy marker the compiler seat put to him, as its D-327:
// a prelude marker trait `Copy`, with impls for every copyable scalar and a
// derivable form for a struct of copyables, landing at the compiler's 1.6.1c,
// after this pin; `Copy` becomes a prelude-owned name, so nothing here is
// called that. At the re-pin that carries it the replacement is MECHANICAL AND
// CONFINED: this block -- the trait and its nine impls -- deleted;
// `vec_at<T: Pod>` bounded `T: Copy`, and its one `pod_copy()` call the copy
// D-327 spells; the umbrella's `Pod` line gone; and the three impls in
// `tests/` -- `vec_at_pod`'s two and `probe19`'s -- in `Copy`'s form. `Pod` is
// used nowhere else, on purpose.
pub trait:Pod = {
    func:pod_copy = Self(Self:self) never fails;
};
impl:int8:Pod = { func:pod_copy = int8(int8:self) never fails { pass self; }; };
impl:int16:Pod = { func:pod_copy = int16(int16:self) never fails { pass self; }; };
impl:int32:Pod = { func:pod_copy = int32(int32:self) never fails { pass self; }; };
impl:int64:Pod = { func:pod_copy = int64(int64:self) never fails { pass self; }; };
impl:uint8:Pod = { func:pod_copy = uint8(uint8:self) never fails { pass self; }; };
impl:uint16:Pod = { func:pod_copy = uint16(uint16:self) never fails { pass self; }; };
impl:uint32:Pod = { func:pod_copy = uint32(uint32:self) never fails { pass self; }; };
impl:uint64:Pod = { func:pod_copy = uint64(uint64:self) never fails { pass self; }; };
impl:bool:Pod = { func:pod_copy = bool(bool:self) never fails { pass self; }; };

// `requires i >= 0 && i < v.count`
//
// A READ, AND SINCE CYCLE 0.1.3c (PD-45) THE LANGUAGE HOLDS IT TO BEING ONE.
// It hands back a COPY of the element, through `Pod`'s `pod_copy`, which only a
// type that owns nothing can implement as declared (above), so at
// `T = string` the call is `NITPICK-TYPE-017`: *"`string` does not implement
// `Pod`, which `vec_at`'s parameter `T` requires"*. And it takes its `Vec` as
// a LOAN, the language's read-only parameter, where it took `Vec<T>->`: a
// `Vec` owns since the same cycle (the header's last section), and `@` of a
// lent owner is `NITPICK-TYPE-085`, so a pointer reader could not be handed a
// `Vec` its caller holds only on loan -- a by-value parameter, a field of a
// lent struct, another module's `sealed` field (`@` of one is
// `NITPICK-TYPE-079`). As a loan it reads all of them, and a local, as
// `vec_at(v, i)`, and through a pointer as `vec_at(<-p, i)`; and it can write
// none of them, which the compiler checks (`TYPE-085`). Measured,
// `meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.3c.md` §1.5.
//
// THE GUARD IS UNCHANGED (TM-129, `SAFETY.md` S-17c): the index still goes
// through a slice laid over `count`, so an index out of range traps
// `OutOfBounds` at every `T`.
//
// UNTIL CYCLE 0.1.3c THIS WAS `vec_at<T> = T(Vec<T>->:v, int64:i)` ENDING
// `pass s[i];`, AND AT AN OWNING `T` IT REMOVED THE ELEMENT. `pass` of a PLACE
// moves implicitly, so at `T = string` the emitted body stored
// `zeroinitializer` over the slot and called `npk.vacant.<dty>` on it while
// leaving `count` untouched, and a second `vec_at` of the same index returned a
// length-0 string -- exit 11,
// `../../tests/probe/defect/generic_owning_copy/case5_vec_at_destructive.npk`,
// which asserts the refusal now. The language behaving as specified, and the
// reason the row was for a non-owning `T` (TM-136); the bound makes the reason
// the compiler's. (And until cycle 0.0.5 this comment said "at an owning `T` a
// copy is refused by TYPE-046", which was false in both halves.) A cycle that
// needs an owning element lifts TM-132's restriction by a decision, and the
// compiler's own `List<T>` is the shape it would take: no by-value get at all.
pub func:vec_at<T: Pod> = T(Vec<T>:v, int64:i) never fails {
    T[]:s = #wild_slice<T>(v.items, v.count);
    pass raw s[i].pod_copy();
};
```

### 4.2 `src/core/bytes.npk` and the umbrella (PD-46; `Pod` for PD-45)

`body` becomes `hidden`, `bytes_capacity` reads its `cap`, the header's two
paragraphs about the seal gain their dated note, and the list of calls that do
not grow a sink names the new one. The umbrella re-exports `Pod` after `Vec`
and `bytes_capacity` after `bytes_len`, one line each, and its count's tag
moves 57 → 59.

### 4.3 `case5`, its README, and its transcript (PD-45)

`case5`'s first line becomes its new marker and a paragraph saying what moved;
the rest of its header is the 0.0.5 record, kept; its two calls read the `Vec`
as a loan. The directory's README gains a section with the three worlds, and
`TRANSCRIPT.txt` gains them as generated rows — **by the script below at step
3, not by a block**, from the files step 2 writes and from `HEAD`.

```
## `case5`, since cycle 0.1.3c — the destructive read is refused

**`case5_vec_at_destructive.npk` is the one file here that is the library's
own, and it asserts a refusal now** (PD-45): `NITPICK-TYPE-017`, "`string`
does not implement `Pod`, which `vec_at`'s parameter `T` requires", at each of
its two calls. `vec_at` takes `T: Pod`, a trait only a type that owns nothing
can implement as it is declared, so the move out of the slot that this file
measured — `pass` of a place at an owning `T`, which the language specifies —
cannot be written through the library any more. Its two calls read the `Vec`
as a loan, `vec_at::<string>(v, 0i64)`, because `vec_at` takes one since the
same cycle; its header says why, and the rest of its header is its record.

| `case5`, both legs | at `c3bdae2` | at `c970483` |
|---|---|---|
| the text and the library before cycle 0.1.3c — the record | run 11 | run 11 |
| this text, `vec_at` a loan WITHOUT its bound — the control | run 11 | run 11 |
| this text and the library — the marker | refused `NITPICK-TYPE-017` | refused `NITPICK-TYPE-017` |

The middle row is why the refusal is the bound's and not the loan's: a loan
alone still moves the element out of the caller's block, through a
`#wild_slice` over the lent header. `TRANSCRIPT.txt`'s section 5 has all
three rows, generated by `meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.3c.md`'s `transcript.py`.
```

<!-- script: transcript.py -->
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.3c step 3: `case5`'s transcript section, generated from the files rather
than typed (`0.1.3c.md` §4.3). Appends SECTION 5 to
tests/probe/defect/generic_owning_copy/TRANSCRIPT.txt: `case5` in three
worlds, at c3bdae2 and c970483, both legs --

  the record    the file and `src/core/vec.npk` as they stand at HEAD, which is
                this plan's commit: `vec_at<T>(Vec<T>->:v, ...)` ending
                `pass s[i];`, and the calls written `@v`
  the control   the file as it stands after step 2, against `vec_at` taking
                its loan with NO bound -- the tree's `vec.npk` with `: Pod`
                removed and `pass raw s[i].pod_copy();` put back to `pass s[i];`
  the marker    the file and the library as they stand after step 2

-- and checks each row against what §1 measured. `vec.npk` imports nothing, so
each world is that one module and the case, laid out at their relative depth
under $W/tx5/<world>/. It refuses to run over its own section a second time,
and writes nothing then. Sources are read and written as BYTES; paths print
relative to the repository, so the transcript names no machine."""
import os, re, subprocess, sys

REPO, WB, W = os.environ["REPO"], os.environ["WB"], os.environ["W"]
PINS = ("c3bdae2", "c970483")
CASE = "tests/probe/defect/generic_owning_copy/case5_vec_at_destructive.npk"
VEC = "src/core/vec.npk"
TX = "tests/probe/defect/generic_owning_copy/TRANSCRIPT.txt"
MARK = "SECTION 5 -- cycle 0.1.3c"
WANT = {"the record": ("run:11", "run:11"), "the control": ("run:11", "run:11"),
        "the marker": ("npkc:NITPICK-TYPE-017", "npkc:NITPICK-TYPE-017")}


def tool(pin, name):
    return os.path.join(WB, ".internal", "toolchain", pin, name)


def rd(path):
    with open(path, "rb") as fh:
        return fh.read()


def wr(path, data):
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(path), exist_ok=True)
    with open(path, "wb") as fh:
        fh.write(data)


def head(rel):
    return subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, "show", "HEAD:" + rel], stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                          check=True).stdout


def verdict(pin, src, leg):
    stem = os.path.basename(src)[:-4]
    d = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(src), "%s.%s" % (pin, leg))
    os.makedirs(d, exist_ok=True)
    ll, obj, exe = (os.path.join(d, stem + x) for x in (".ll", ".o", ""))
    p = subprocess.run([tool(pin, "npkc"), os.path.basename(src), "-o", ll],
                       cwd=os.path.dirname(src), stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT)
    if p.returncode != 0 or not os.path.isfile(ll):
        found = sorted(set(re.findall(r"NITPICK-[A-Z]+-\d+", p.stdout.decode("utf-8", "replace"))))
        return "npkc:" + ",".join(found)
    if leg == "O2":
        if subprocess.run(["opt", "-O2", "-S", ll, "-o", ll + ".opt"]).returncode:
            return "opt"
        ll, flag = ll + ".opt", "-O2"
    else:
        flag = "-O0"
    if subprocess.run(["llc", flag, "-filetype=obj", "-relocation-model=static", ll,
                       "-o", obj]).returncode:
        return "llc"
    if subprocess.run(["ld.lld", "-static", obj, tool(pin, "npkrt.o"), "-o", exe]).returncode:
        return "ld"
    r = subprocess.run(["env", "-i", exe], stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
    return "run:%d" % (r.returncode if r.returncode >= 0 else 128 - r.returncode)


path = os.path.join(REPO, TX)
old = rd(path).decode("utf-8")
if MARK in old:
    sys.exit("STOP: %s already carries SECTION 5; nothing written" % TX)
tree_vec = rd(os.path.join(REPO, VEC))
LOAN_AT = b"pub func:vec_at<T: Pod> = T(Vec<T>:v, int64:i) never fails {"
BODY_AT = b"    pass raw s[i].pod_copy();\n};"
for old_text in (LOAN_AT, BODY_AT):
    if tree_vec.count(old_text) != 1:
        sys.exit("STOP: the tree's vec.npk does not hold `vec_at` as step 2 writes it")
nobound = tree_vec.replace(LOAN_AT, LOAN_AT.replace(b"<T: Pod>", b"<T>")).replace(
    BODY_AT, b"    pass s[i];\n};")
worlds = [("the record", head(VEC), head(CASE)),
          ("the control", nobound, rd(os.path.join(REPO, CASE))),
          ("the marker", tree_vec, rd(os.path.join(REPO, CASE)))]
rows, bad = [], 0
for name, vec, case in worlds:
    root = os.path.join(W, "tx5", name.split()[1])
    wr(os.path.join(root, VEC), vec)
    wr(os.path.join(root, CASE), case)
    for pin in PINS:
        v0 = verdict(pin, os.path.join(root, CASE), "O0")
        v2 = v0 if v0.startswith("npkc") else verdict(pin, os.path.join(root, CASE), "O2")
        rows.append("%-12s %-8s %-26s %s" % (name, pin, v0, v2))
        if (v0, v2) != WANT[name]:
            bad += 1
text = ("\n%s: case5's marker moves to NITPICK-TYPE-017 (PD-45).\n"
        "Appended by `meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.3c.md`'s transcript.py; every line above is\n"
        "unchanged. case5 in three worlds, at both pins the workbench keeps from 1.5 on:\n\n"
        "  the record    the file and src/core/vec.npk as they stood before cycle 0.1.3c --\n"
        "                `vec_at<T>(Vec<T>->:v, int64:i)` ending `pass s[i];`, calls `@v`\n"
        "  the control   the file as it stands, against `vec_at` taking its loan with NO\n"
        "                bound -- `vec_at<T>(Vec<T>:v, int64:i)` ending `pass s[i];`\n"
        "  the marker    the file and the library as they stand: `vec_at<T: Pod>`\n\n"
        "%-12s %-8s %-26s %s\n%s\n\n"
        "So the destructive read runs at both pins until the bound, and the loan alone\n"
        "does not close it: through a `#wild_slice` over the lent header, `pass s[i]` still\n"
        "moves the element out of the caller's block.\n\n"
        "END OF SECTION 5\n" % (MARK, "world", "pin", "-O0", "opt -O2", "\n".join(rows)))
with open(path, "ab") as fh:
    fh.write(text.encode("utf-8"))
print(text, end="")
print("rows as section 1 of the plan measured them: %d of %d" % (len(rows) - bad, len(rows)))
sys.exit(0 if bad == 0 else 1)
```

### 4.4 The new tests (PD-44 … PD-47)

Eleven files. **The churn pair** first (§1.7): each header's markers are the
statements its twin fails, derived from the source before measured. Then **the
four library refusals** — `probe16f`, `probe16g`, `probe16h` for the move-only
property and `probe16i` for `Bytes.body` — each one shape, refused once, with a
`failsafe` naming a superset of what `NITPICK-REACH-002` asks, as `probe16`'s
family does, so a reordered compiler cannot add a stray `REACH-002` to the
refusal; **the three language probes** — `probe18`, `probe18b`, `probe19`; and
**the two positive twins**, `vec_moves` and `vec_at_pod`. Every file that runs
names exactly the arms `NITPICK-REACH-002` asks and nothing more (0.1.0b's
rule), measured file by file at planning; every exit code a file chooses is
clear of every arm code (TM-152).

<!-- apply code: new tests/unit/vec_churn_pop.npk -->
```
// expect-exit: 0
// heap: count >= 2000000
// heap: peak_live <= 75000
// cap: 65536 KiB, exit 0
//
// `vec_pop` AT AN OWNING `T` HANDS THE ELEMENT OVER -- TM-150's churn pair, the
// REMEDY HALF, committed at cycle 0.1.3c (PD-47).
//
// ASKS: two million cycles of `vec_push` then `vec_pop` on one `Vec<string>`,
// each popped string bound and dropped at the bottom of its iteration -- does
// anything accumulate?
//
// MATTERS BECAUSE `vec_pop` is the one element-removing verb in
// `src/core/vec.npk` that is NOT among the four that orphan an owning element
// (`SAFETY.md` S-18d): its `move` hands the element to the caller, whose binding
// drops it. TM-150 measured that at cycle 0.1.0b in a scratch directory -- 120
// bytes at the peak against `vec_clear`'s 48 000 096 -- and quoted the pair in
// `vec.npk`, S-18d and the workbench's `PLAYBOOK.md` with no program behind
// it: a number whose program is not committed is a claim, not evidence (the
// playbook §6). This is the program, and its twin is `vec_churn_clear.npk`,
// which differs in the one operation that ends each cycle.
//
// THE THREE MARKERS ARE EACH A STATEMENT ITS TWIN FAILS (`TESTING.md` V-17).
// `count >= 2000000`: the loop made its two million bodies, so a copy whose
// loop was emptied -- which retains nothing because it allocates nothing -- is
// red. `peak_live <= 75000`: none of them was retained, where the twin retains
// every one. And exit 0 under a 64 MiB cap, where the twin takes 92. Derived
// from the source before it was measured: the block is `vec_init(4)` of
// 24-byte `string` headers, 96 bytes, allocated once and never grown, since
// `count` never passes 1; each cycle allocates one 24-byte body --
// `"Europe/Kyiv_"` and `"0123456789ab"`, twelve bytes each -- and drops it
// before the next, so the peak is 96 + 24 = 120 and the count 1 + 2 000 000.
// Measured at compiler `c970483` and at `c3bdae2`, both legs: `allocated=48000096
// peak_live=120 count=2000001`. The ceiling is TM-185's rule: the geometric
// mean of 120 and the twin's 48 000 096, 75 894, rounded down to two
// significant figures. By hand: `NPK_HEAP_STATS=1 <binary>`.
//
// EXIT CODES, clear of every arm code (TM-152): 10 a popped string is not the
// one pushed; 11 the `Vec` is not empty after the last pop.
mod:vec_churn_pop;

use "../../src/core/vec.npk".*;

func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    Vec<string>:v = raw vec_init::<string>(4i64);
    int64:i = 0i64;
    while (i < 2000000i64) decreases 2000000i64 - i {
        drop vec_push(@v, string_concat("Europe/Kyiv_", "0123456789ab"));
        string:s = raw vec_pop(@v);
        if (string_byte_length(s) != 24i64) { exit 10i32; }
        i = i + 1i64;
    }
    if (v.count != 0i64) { exit 11i32; }
    drop vec_free(@v);
    exit 0i32;
};

func:failsafe = int32(Error:e) {
    pick (e) {
        (HeapBadRequest)    { exit 91i32; },
        (HeapOom)           { exit 92i32; },
        (IntOverflow)       { exit 93i32; },
        (OutOfBounds)       { exit 94i32; },
        (Unreachable)       { exit 95i32; },
        (WildLeak)          { exit 96i32; },
        (StackExhausted)    { exit 106i32; },
        (MachineFault)      { exit 107i32; },
        (DecreasesViolated) { exit 108i32; },
        (LimitViolated)     { exit 109i32; },
        (*)                 { exit 99i32; }
    }
    exit 9i32;
};
```

```
// expect-exit: 0
// heap: peak_live >= 48000000

//
// NOT A TEST OF SOMETHING WORKING. This file exits 0 and that IS the finding:
// `vec_clear` at an owning `T` orphans every element it discards, and the
// language's own leak gate does not notice.
//
// `vec_clear` AT AN OWNING `T` -- TM-150's churn pair, the LEAKING HALF,
// committed at cycle 0.1.3c (PD-47).
//
// ASKS: the same two million cycles as `vec_churn_pop.npk`, with `vec_clear`
// where the pop is -- what does it cost?

// MATTERS BECAUSE `vec_clear` is one of the four operations `SAFETY.md` S-18d

// drop, and `exit 0` cannot see it -- D-151 counts `wild` blocks, and a
// `string`'s body is managed (TM-106). Every one of the two million bodies is
// still live at exit. The day a cycle gives `vec_clear` an element drop, this
// file goes red, and the restriction's reason has to be re-read.
//
// ITS TWIN IS `vec_churn_pop.npk`, which differs in the one operation that
// ends each cycle. `peak_live >= 48000000` is the leak's arithmetic -- two
// million 24-byte bodies, every one live at the peak -- so it is also this
// repository's second known-leaking control for the instrument (`TESTING.md`

// both legs: `allocated=48000096 peak_live=48000096 count=2000001` -- the 96-byte

mod:vec_churn_clear;

};

func:failsafe = int32(Error:e) {

        (HeapBadRequest)    { exit 91i32; },
        (HeapOom)           { exit 92i32; },
        (IntOverflow)       { exit 93i32; },
        (OutOfBounds)       { exit 94i32; },
        (Unreachable)       { exit 95i32; },
        (WildLeak)          { exit 96i32; },
        (StackExhausted)    { exit 106i32; },

        (DecreasesViolated) { exit 108i32; },
```

```
// expect-error: NITPICK-TYPE-046
//
// PROBE 16f — A CONSUMER CANNOT COPY A `Vec`. The whole-struct copy the seal
// did not stop, asserted by a refusal.
//
// ASKS: may code outside `src/core/vec.npk` bind a second `Vec` to the first,
// `Vec<int64>:w = v;`?
//
// MATTERS BECAUSE a copy of a `Vec` is a second handle on one `wild` block:
// the two headers free it, grow it and read it independently, so after
// `vec_free(@v)` everything done through `w` is done to freed memory. Cycle
// 0.1.0c measured it and hiding and sealing could not stop it -- a copy names
// no field (TM-156) -- and the workbench's question 9 took it to the author,
// who chose MOVE-ONLY BY CONSTRUCTION on 2026-09-25.
//
// ANSWER, measured at `c970483` and `c3bdae2` (cycle 0.1.3c, PD-44): NO. `Vec`'s
// last field is `hidden string[0]:move_only`, so the type owns and the copy
// below is `NITPICK-TYPE-046`, exactly once: "`Vec<int64>` owns storage that is
// released at scope exit, so it is move-only". THE REFUSAL IS THE ASSERTION
// (B-7). ITS CONTROL is this file against the library before the marker: it
// compiles, links, and the pop through `w` reads the allocator's free poison,
// exit 70 on both legs (`meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.3c.md` §1.4). `probe16g` is the
// same hole by assignment, `probe16h` by a struct that holds a `Vec`, and
// `../unit/vec_moves.npk` is the positive twin -- the moves and loans that
// still compile.
mod:probe16f_vec_copy_refused;

use "../../src/core/vec.npk".*;

func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    Vec<int64>:v = raw vec_init::<int64>(4i64);
    drop vec_push(@v, 7i64);
    Vec<int64>:w = v;
    drop vec_free(@v);
    int64:x = raw vec_pop(@w);
    if (x != 7i64) { exit 70i32; }
    drop vec_free(@w);
    exit 0i32;
};

// A SUPERSET of what `NITPICK-REACH-002` asks, deliberately (`SAFETY.md` S-6b,
// constraint 3: a superset compiles). If a later compiler reordered its phases
// so that the reach analysis ran before the check this file asserts, a
// failsafe naming exactly the demanded arms could add a stray `REACH-002` to
// the refusal; this one cannot.
func:failsafe = int32(Error:e) {
    pick (e) {
        (HeapBadRequest)    { exit 91i32; },
        (HeapOom)           { exit 92i32; },
        (IntOverflow)       { exit 93i32; },
        (OutOfBounds)       { exit 94i32; },
        (Unreachable)       { exit 95i32; },
        (WildLeak)          { exit 96i32; },
        (DivByZero)         { exit 97i32; },
        (DivOverflow)       { exit 98i32; },
        (StackExhausted)    { exit 106i32; },
        (MachineFault)      { exit 107i32; },
        (DecreasesViolated) { exit 108i32; },
        (LimitViolated)     { exit 109i32; },
        (*)                 { exit 99i32; }
    }
    exit 9i32;
};
```

```
// expect-error: NITPICK-TYPE-046
//
// PROBE 16g — A CONSUMER CANNOT ASSIGN ONE `Vec` OVER ANOTHER. The same
// second handle as `probe16f`, reached by assignment rather than by a new
// binding, asserted by a refusal.
//
// ASKS: may code outside `src/core/vec.npk` write `v = u;` of two `Vec`s?
//
// MATTERS BECAUSE the assignment overwrites all three of `v`'s fields at once
// and names none of them, so no seal sees it (TM-156 recorded it beside the
// copy): `v` and `u` then share `u`'s block, and `v`'s own block is lost.
//
// ANSWER, measured at `c970483` and `c3bdae2` (cycle 0.1.3c, PD-44): NO -- the
// assignment below is `NITPICK-TYPE-046`, exactly once, because a `Vec` owns
// and its copy is refused whatever the copy is written as. THE REFUSAL IS THE
// ASSERTION (B-7). ITS CONTROL is this file against the library before the
// marker: it compiles and the pop through `v` reads the free poison, exit 70
// on both legs. A transfer is `v = move(u);` (`../unit/vec_moves.npk`).
mod:probe16g_vec_assign_refused;

use "../../src/core/vec.npk".*;

func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    Vec<int64>:u = raw vec_init::<int64>(4i64);
    Vec<int64>:v = raw vec_init::<int64>(4i64);
    drop vec_push(@u, 7i64);
    v = u;
    drop vec_free(@u);
    int64:x = raw vec_pop(@v);
    if (x != 7i64) { exit 70i32; }
    exit 0i32;
};

// A SUPERSET of what `NITPICK-REACH-002` asks, deliberately (`SAFETY.md` S-6b,
// constraint 3: a superset compiles). If a later compiler reordered its phases
// so that the reach analysis ran before the check this file asserts, a
// failsafe naming exactly the demanded arms could add a stray `REACH-002` to
// the refusal; this one cannot.
func:failsafe = int32(Error:e) {
    pick (e) {
        (HeapBadRequest)    { exit 91i32; },
        (HeapOom)           { exit 92i32; },
        (IntOverflow)       { exit 93i32; },
        (OutOfBounds)       { exit 94i32; },
        (Unreachable)       { exit 95i32; },
        (WildLeak)          { exit 96i32; },
```

```
// expect-error: NITPICK-TYPE-046
//
// PROBE 16h — A CONSUMER CANNOT COPY A STRUCT THAT HOLDS A `Vec`. The second
// handle one level down, asserted by a refusal.
//
// ASKS: may a consumer copy a struct of its own whose field is a `Vec`?
//
// MATTERS BECAUSE this is the shape a `Vec` is actually used in: cycle 0.4's
// `Layout` holds a `Vec` of its parts (`FORMAT_MODEL.md` §3), and a copy of
// such a struct copies the `Vec`'s header with it -- the same second handle as
// `probe16f`, with nothing at the copy site that says `Vec`.
//
// ANSWER, measured at `c970483` and `c3bdae2` (cycle 0.1.3c, PD-44): NO. A
// struct owns when any field owns (the compiler's D-183), so `Holder` is
// move-only by containment and the copy below is `NITPICK-TYPE-046`, exactly
// once. THE REFUSAL IS THE ASSERTION (B-7). ITS CONTROL is this file against
// the library before the marker: it compiles and the pop through the copy
// reads the free poison, exit 70 on both legs. Moving such a struct is
// `../unit/vec_moves.npk`'s.
mod:probe16h_vec_holder_copy_refused;

use "../../src/core/vec.npk".*;

struct:Holder = { Vec<int64>:v; int64:tag; };

func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    Vec<int64>:a = raw vec_init::<int64>(4i64);
    drop vec_push(@a, 7i64);
    Holder:h = Holder{ v: move(a), tag: 1i64 };
    Holder:k = h;
    drop vec_free(@h.v);
    int64:x = raw vec_pop(@k.v);
    if (x != 7i64) { exit 70i32; }
    exit 0i32;
};

// A SUPERSET of what `NITPICK-REACH-002` asks, deliberately (`SAFETY.md` S-6b,
// constraint 3: a superset compiles). If a later compiler reordered its phases
// so that the reach analysis ran before the check this file asserts, a
// failsafe naming exactly the demanded arms could add a stray `REACH-002` to
// the refusal; this one cannot.
func:failsafe = int32(Error:e) {
    pick (e) {
        (HeapBadRequest)    { exit 91i32; },
        (HeapOom)           { exit 92i32; },
        (IntOverflow)       { exit 93i32; },
        (OutOfBounds)       { exit 94i32; },
        (Unreachable)       { exit 95i32; },
        (WildLeak)          { exit 96i32; },
        (DivByZero)         { exit 97i32; },
        (DivOverflow)       { exit 98i32; },
        (StackExhausted)    { exit 106i32; },
        (MachineFault)      { exit 107i32; },
        (DecreasesViolated) { exit 108i32; },
        (LimitViolated)     { exit 109i32; },
        (*)                 { exit 99i32; }
    }
    exit 9i32;
};
```

```
// expect-error: NITPICK-TYPE-080
//
// PROBE 16i — A CONSUMER CANNOT WRITE THROUGH `Bytes.body`. What the seal did
// not stop, asserted by a refusal.
//
// ASKS: may code outside `src/core/bytes.npk` write a sink's bytes through its
// body's pointer, `b.body.ptr[0i64] = 65u8;`?
//
// MATTERS BECAUSE `sealed` kept the FIELD's own bytes -- the `buffer` header --
// and the compiler's D-313 lets a write THROUGH a sealed pointer field pass:
// measured at `c3bdae2` (cycle 0.1.0c, TM-156), a consumer's write compiled,
// ran, and `bytes_view` then read 65 where the sink had written 97; and the
// index is a bare pointer's (TM-108), so a write one byte past the allocation
// ran at exit 0 with no trap. The workbench's question 9 carried it to the
// author with the `Vec` copy.
//
// ANSWER, measured at `c970483` and `c3bdae2` (cycle 0.1.3c, PD-46): NO. `body`
// is `hidden` (the compiler's D-314), so outside its module the member access
// is `NITPICK-TYPE-080`, exactly once, and a consumer reads the capacity
// through `bytes_capacity` (`probe16e`). THE REFUSAL IS THE ASSERTION (B-7).

};
```
