<!-- apply code: new tests/probe/probe18_zero_length_owner.npk -->
```
// expect-exit: 0
//
// PROBE 18 — A ZERO-LENGTH ARRAY OF AN OWNING TYPE IS A FIELD THAT HOLDS
// NOTHING, OCCUPIES NOTHING, AND MAKES ITS STRUCT MOVE-ONLY. The language fact
// `Vec`'s move-only property rests on (cycle 0.1.3c, PD-44; `SAFETY.md` S-18g),
// asked with no library code at all.
//
// ASKS: does a `string[0]` field cost any bytes, does a struct holding one
// move and drop cleanly, and does the same field over `int64[0]` leave its
// struct copyable?
//
// MATTERS BECAUSE `src/core/vec.npk`'s `move_only` field is this shape, and if
// a later compiler refused `T[0]`, gave it a size, or stopped counting its
// element's ownership, the four `Vec` fixtures would fail at once with nothing
// naming the cause. This probe names it. WHY THE LANGUAGE DOES IT, as
// `nitpick-regex` read it in the compiler at `c3bdae2` (its probe 16, RX-161):
// the compiler answers whether an ARRAY owns by its element, whatever the
// length, and marks a struct owning when any field does (D-183); an owning type
// is move-only (`TYPE-046`); the array's size is its element's times zero; and
// `[]` is the zero array (D-139). The compiler's `TYPE_REFERENCE.md` §9.2
// states `T[0]` supported since 1.6.0 step 3g.
//
// ANSWER, measured at `c970483` and `c3bdae2`, both legs: YES to all three.
// Each is its own exit, none an arm code (TM-152): 10 `Marked` is not 24 bytes;
// 11 `Plain` is not; 12 and 13 `Plain`'s copy, or its original, reads wrong --
// the `int64[0]` field owns nothing, so the copy is legal; 14 a thousand
// `Marked` values built, moved and dropped did not sum right -- the drop the
// compiler generates walks no element and frees nothing. Its refused twin is
// `probe18b_zero_length_owner_copy_refused.npk`, the copy of `Marked`.
mod:probe18_zero_length_owner;

struct:Marked = {
    int64:a;
    int64:b;
    int64:c;
    string[0]:move_only;
};

struct:Plain = {
    int64:a;
    int64:b;
    int64:c;
    int64[0]:nothing;
};

func:hold = int64(int64:k) never fails {
    Marked:m = Marked{ a: k, b: k, c: k, move_only: [] };
    Marked:n = move(m);
    pass n.c;
};

func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    if (#size_of<Marked>() != 24i64) { exit 10i32; }
    if (#size_of<Plain>() != 24i64)  { exit 11i32; }
    Plain:p = Plain{ a: 1i64, b: 2i64, c: 3i64, nothing: [] };
    Plain:q = p;
    if (q.c != 3i64) { exit 12i32; }
    if (p.c != 3i64) { exit 13i32; }
    int64:i = 0i64;
    int64:sum = 0i64;
    while (i < 1000i64) decreases 1000i64 - i {
        sum = sum + (raw hold(i));
        i = i + 1i64;
    }
    if (sum != 499500i64) { exit 14i32; }
    exit 0i32;
};

func:failsafe = int32(Error:e) {
    pick (e) {
        (HeapBadRequest)    { exit 91i32; },
        (HeapOom)           { exit 92i32; },
        (IntOverflow)       { exit 93i32; },
        (Unreachable)       { exit 95i32; },
        (WildLeak)          { exit 96i32; },
        (StackExhausted)    { exit 106i32; },
        (MachineFault)      { exit 107i32; },
        (DecreasesViolated) { exit 108i32; },
        (*)                 { exit 99i32; }
    }
    exit 9i32;
};
```

// expect-error: NITPICK-TYPE-047
//
// PROBE 19 — AN OWNING TYPE CANNOT IMPLEMENT `Pod` AS IT IS DECLARED. The claim
// `vec_at`'s bound rests on (cycle 0.1.3c, PD-45; `SAFETY.md` S-18h).
//
// ASKS: may a consumer declare `string` a `Pod`, writing `pod_copy` as the
// trait declares it -- `self` lent, `pass self`?
//
// MATTERS BECAUSE `vec_at<T: Pod>` hands an element back by value, and the
// bound is worth something only if the LANGUAGE decides who may implement it.
// If this compiled, `vec_at` at `T = string` would hand back a second owner of
// the element -- the destructive read `defect/generic_owning_copy/case5`
// measured, turned into a double free.
//
// ANSWER, measured at `c970483` and `c3bdae2`: NO. `pass self` of a lent owner
// is `NITPICK-TYPE-047` -- "this parameter was lent, not given" -- exactly once.
// THE REFUSAL IS THE ASSERTION (B-7). The positive twin is
// `../unit/vec_at_pod.npk`: a type that owns nothing implements it in one line.
//
// WHAT THIS DOES NOT STOP, and says so: `TYPE-047`'s message suggests declaring
// the parameter `move`, and at `c970483` an impl that does is ACCEPTED, though
// the trait lends it -- the compiler's DEF-116, fixed at no pin of ours. Such
// an impl makes `vec_at` a double free (measured, `meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.3c.md`
// §1.6). This library writes none; `nitpick-regex` pins the shape.
mod:probe19_pod_owner_refused;

use "../../src/core/vec.npk".*;

impl:string:Pod = { func:pod_copy = string(string:self) never fails { pass self; }; };

func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    exit 0i32;
};

// A SUPERSET of what `NITPICK-REACH-002` asks, deliberately (`SAFETY.md` S-6b,
// constraint 3: a superset compiles). If a later compiler reordered its phases
// so that the reach analysis ran before the check this file asserts, a
// failsafe naming exactly the demanded arms could add a stray `REACH-002` to
// the refusal; this one cannot.
func:failsafe = int32(Error:e) {
    pick (e) {
        (HeapBadRequest)    { exit 91i32; },
        (HeapOom)           { exit 92i32; },
        (IntOverflow)       { exit 93i32; },
        (OutOfBounds)       { exit 94i32; },
        (Unreachable)       { exit 95i32; },
        (WildLeak)          { exit 96i32; },
        (DivByZero)         { exit 97i32; },
        (DivOverflow)       { exit 98i32; },
        (StackExhausted)    { exit 106i32; },
        (MachineFault)      { exit 107i32; },
        (DecreasesViolated) { exit 108i32; },
        (LimitViolated)     { exit 109i32; },
        (*)                 { exit 99i32; }
    }

};

<!-- apply code: new tests/unit/vec_moves.npk -->
```
// expect-exit: 0
//
// WHAT A MOVE-ONLY `Vec` STILL DOES -- the positive twin of `probe16f`, `probe16g`
// and `probe16h` (cycle 0.1.3c, PD-44 and PD-45; `SAFETY.md` S-18g).
//
// A refusal proves nothing on its own: those three would pass against a
// compiler that refused every use of a `Vec`, and that compiler would break
// every consumer. So this file runs each shape the property still allows, and
// a wrong answer exits with its own code, none an arm code (TM-152):
//
//   10, 11  a LOAN, read two ways: a by-value parameter reads its `Vec` through
//           `vec_at` without `@` -- a loan of an owner is read-only, and `@v`
//           of one is `NITPICK-TYPE-085` -- and a pointer lends its pointee,
//           `vec_at(<-p, 0i64)`;
//   12      nothing moved: the loans left the count alone;
//   13      a whole `Vec` MOVED to a new binding, `move(v)`;
//   14      a struct built by moving a `Vec` into it, read through a loan of
//           the STRUCT -- `vec_at(h.v, 0i64)`, a field of a lent struct;
//   15      that struct MOVED in turn;
//   16      a `move Vec<int64>:v` parameter TAKES ownership and frees the block,
//           once -- the caller wrote `move(...)`;
//   17      an element-by-element COPY into a fresh `Vec`, made before the
//           original was moved away and freed, still reads its own elements
//           -- the one copy that stays legal, because it copies ELEMENTS, and
//           each is an `int64`.
//
// And `exit 0` asserts, through D-151, that both blocks were freed exactly once:
// the drop the compiler generates for a `Vec` since the marker frees nothing,
// so a block `vec_free` missed would trap `WildLeak` here.
mod:vec_moves;

use "../../src/core/vec.npk".*;

struct:Holder = { Vec<int64>:v; int64:tag; };

func:consume = int64(move Vec<int64>:v) never fails {
    int64:n = v.count;
    drop vec_free(@v);
    pass n;
};

func:first = int64(Vec<int64>:v) never fails {
    pass raw vec_at(v, 0i64);
};

func:first_of = int64(Holder:h) never fails {
    pass raw vec_at(h.v, 0i64);
};

func:first_via = int64(Vec<int64>->:p) never fails {
    pass raw vec_at(<-p, 0i64);
};

func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    Vec<int64>:v = raw vec_init::<int64>(4i64);
    drop vec_push(@v, 7i64);
    drop vec_push(@v, 8i64);

    if (raw first(v) != 7i64)        { exit 10i32; }
    if (raw first_via(@v) != 7i64)   { exit 11i32; }
    if (v.count != 2i64)             { exit 12i32; }

    Vec<int64>:c = raw vec_init::<int64>(v.count);
    int64:i = 0i64;
    int64:n = v.count;
    while (i < n) decreases n - i {
        drop vec_push(@c, raw vec_at(v, i));
        i = i + 1i64;
    }

    Vec<int64>:w = move(v);
    if (w.count != 2i64)             { exit 13i32; }
    Holder:h = Holder{ v: move(w), tag: 1i64 };
    if (raw first_of(h) != 7i64)     { exit 14i32; }
    Holder:k = move(h);
    if (k.v.count != 2i64)           { exit 15i32; }
    int64:gone = raw consume(move(k.v));
    if (gone != 2i64)                { exit 16i32; }

    if (raw vec_at(c, 1i64) != 8i64) { exit 17i32; }
    drop vec_free(@c);
    exit 0i32;
};

func:failsafe = int32(Error:e) {
    pick (e) {
        (HeapBadRequest)    { exit 91i32; },
        (HeapOom)           { exit 92i32; },
        (IntOverflow)       { exit 93i32; },
        (OutOfBounds)       { exit 94i32; },
        (Unreachable)       { exit 95i32; },
        (WildLeak)          { exit 96i32; },
        (StackExhausted)    { exit 106i32; },
        (MachineFault)      { exit 107i32; },
        (DecreasesViolated) { exit 108i32; },
        (LimitViolated)     { exit 109i32; },
        (*)                 { exit 99i32; }
    }
    exit 9i32;
};
```

<!-- apply code: new tests/unit/vec_at_pod.npk -->
```
// expect-exit: 0
//
// `vec_at<T: Pod>` FOR A CONSUMER'S OWN TYPES, THROUGH THE UMBRELLA -- the
// positive twin of `probe19_pod_owner_refused.npk` (cycle 0.1.3c, PD-45;
// `SAFETY.md` S-18h).
//
// A bound that nothing can satisfy would pass that probe too, so this file
// implements `Pod` the way a consumer would -- in one line beside each type's
// declaration, importing `src/lib.npk` and nothing else, so the trait's
// re-export is under test as well -- and reads every element TWICE: a read
// that moved the element out would fail the second (`generic_owning_copy/case5`
// measured exactly that until this cycle). Each wrong answer exits with its
// own code, none an arm code (TM-152):
//
//   10, 11  a struct of two integers, read twice at one index;
//   12      the reads left the count alone;
//   13, 14  a payload enum -- `FORMAT_MODEL.md`'s parts in miniature, the
//           element type cycle 0.4's `Layout` will hold -- read twice, the
//           payload intact;
//   20 ... 28  each of the nine scalar impls `src/core/vec.npk` declares,
//           read once, so a mistyped impl is found here and not by the first
//           consumer that instantiates it.
mod:vec_at_pod;

use "../../src/lib.npk".*;

struct:Pt = { int64:x; int64:y; };
impl:Pt:Pod = { func:pod_copy = Pt(Pt:self) never fails { pass self; }; };

enum:Part = {
    Literal(uint16);
    Year4;
    Month2;
    Day2;
};
impl:Part:Pod = { func:pod_copy = Part(Part:self) never fails { pass self; }; };

func:width = int64(Part:p) never fails {
    int64:w = 0i64;
    pick (p) {
        (Part.Literal(ix)) { w = 1000i64 + (ix => int64); },
        (Part.Year4)       { w = 4i64; },
        (Part.Month2)      { w = 2i64; },
        (Part.Day2)        { w = 2i64; }
    }
    pass w;
};

func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    Vec<Pt>:pts = raw vec_init::<Pt>(2i64);
    drop vec_push(@pts, Pt{ x: 3i64, y: 4i64 });
    Pt:a = raw vec_at(pts, 0i64);
    Pt:b = raw vec_at(pts, 0i64);
    if (a.x != 3i64 || a.y != 4i64)  { exit 10i32; }
    if (b.x != 3i64 || b.y != 4i64)  { exit 11i32; }
    if (pts.count != 1i64)           { exit 12i32; }
    drop vec_free(@pts);

    Vec<Part>:parts = raw vec_init::<Part>(2i64);
    drop vec_push(@parts, Part.Year4);
    drop vec_push(@parts, Part.Literal(45u16));
    Part:c = raw vec_at(parts, 1i64);
    Part:d = raw vec_at(parts, 1i64);
    if ((raw width(c)) != 1045i64)   { exit 13i32; }
    if ((raw width(d)) != 1045i64)   { exit 14i32; }
    drop vec_free(@parts);

    Vec<int8>:i8s = raw vec_init::<int8>(1i64);
    drop vec_push(@i8s, 0i8 - 8i8);
    if (raw vec_at(i8s, 0i64) != 0i8 - 8i8)        { exit 20i32; }
    drop vec_free(@i8s);
    Vec<int16>:i16s = raw vec_init::<int16>(1i64);
    drop vec_push(@i16s, 0i16 - 16i16);
    if (raw vec_at(i16s, 0i64) != 0i16 - 16i16)    { exit 21i32; }
    drop vec_free(@i16s);
    Vec<int32>:i32s = raw vec_init::<int32>(1i64);
    drop vec_push(@i32s, 0i32 - 32i32);
    if (raw vec_at(i32s, 0i64) != 0i32 - 32i32)    { exit 22i32; }
    drop vec_free(@i32s);
    Vec<int64>:i64s = raw vec_init::<int64>(1i64);
    drop vec_push(@i64s, 0i64 - 64i64);
    if (raw vec_at(i64s, 0i64) != 0i64 - 64i64)    { exit 23i32; }
    drop vec_free(@i64s);
    Vec<uint8>:u8s = raw vec_init::<uint8>(1i64);
    drop vec_push(@u8s, 208u8);
    if (raw vec_at(u8s, 0i64) != 208u8)            { exit 24i32; }
    drop vec_free(@u8s);
    Vec<uint16>:u16s = raw vec_init::<uint16>(1i64);
    drop vec_push(@u16s, 60016u16);
    if (raw vec_at(u16s, 0i64) != 60016u16)        { exit 25i32; }
    drop vec_free(@u16s);
    Vec<uint32>:u32s = raw vec_init::<uint32>(1i64);
    drop vec_push(@u32s, 4000000032u32);
    if (raw vec_at(u32s, 0i64) != 4000000032u32)   { exit 26i32; }
    drop vec_free(@u32s);
    Vec<uint64>:u64s = raw vec_init::<uint64>(1i64);
    drop vec_push(@u64s, ~0u64);
    if (raw vec_at(u64s, 0i64) != ~0u64) { exit 27i32; }
    drop vec_free(@u64s);
    Vec<bool>:bs = raw vec_init::<bool>(1i64);
    drop vec_push(@bs, true);
    if (!(raw vec_at(bs, 0i64)))                   { exit 28i32; }
    drop vec_free(@bs);
    exit 0i32;
};

func:failsafe = int32(Error:e) {
    pick (e) {
        (ETimeValue)        { exit 87i32; },
        (HeapBadRequest)    { exit 91i32; },
        (HeapOom)           { exit 92i32; },
        (IntOverflow)       { exit 93i32; },
        (OutOfBounds)       { exit 94i32; },
        (Unreachable)       { exit 95i32; },
        (WildLeak)          { exit 96i32; },
        (DivByZero)         { exit 97i32; },
        (DivOverflow)       { exit 98i32; },
        (StackExhausted)    { exit 106i32; },
        (MachineFault)      { exit 107i32; },
        (DecreasesViolated) { exit 108i32; },
        (LimitViolated)     { exit 109i32; },
        (*)                 { exit 99i32; }
    }
    exit 9i32;
};
```

### 4.5 The tests that move: thirteen loans, sixteen capacity reads

`vec_boundaries` and `vec_at_past_end` call `vec_at` with the `Vec` itself —
`v`, not `@v` — and say why once each; `probe16e`, `bytes_growth` and
`bytes_view_lifetime` read the capacity through `bytes_capacity`, `probe16e`'s
header saying what changed and `probe16c`'s pointing at `probe16i`. No
assertion in any of them changes, and the two heap-bounded files measure what
they did. (`probe16c`'s header grows by two lines, so its one diagnostic is
two lines lower, `27:5` for `25:5`; it asserts the code alone, and step 6's
sweep shows the move.)

## 5. The documents, and the decisions

Groups `docs` and `decisions`, applied at step 4, after the transcript: the
specifications, the open question, `CLAUDE.md`, three roadmap READMEs, and
`DECISIONS.md`. Every block is a dated amendment or a new rule; no settled
text is rewritten (TM-002's rule and the D-085/D-202 pattern).

### 5.1 The specifications — `SAFETY.md`, `BUILD.md`, `TESTING.md`

`SAFETY.md`: §1's move-only row — **the reason-sweep's finding (§6.2)**: it
said the language *"does not stop the move that faults"*, O-N20's hazard stated
as current in a live rule, with no id for a sweep to find — now says what
`c970483` does, with the history beside it, and names the new property; S-17b
and S-17c gain dated notes (`Bytes.body` hidden; `vec_at`'s `pass`); S-18d
gains a dated amendment (the destructive read refused, the churn pair
committed); and **S-18g** (move-only) and **S-18h** (`Pod`) are new rules after
S-18f. `BUILD.md`: B-12's two shapes, B-4c's probe split, and §3's parse split
with its history. `TESTING.md`: the parse stage's denominator and V-17's
closing counts.

<!-- apply docs: to meta/specs/SAFETY.md -->
```
consumes the sink by `move` compiles and is correct on both legs; measured,
and carried to `meta/roadmap/0.4/README.md`.

**Rule S-18g (PD-44, cycle 0.1.3c) — `Vec<T>` is move-only by construction,
and so is everything that holds one.** Its last field is
`hidden string[0]:move_only`: a fixed array of no strings, zero bytes, whose
ELEMENT owns — so the compiler marks every `Vec<T>` owning (its D-183), and a
copy of an owner is `NITPICK-TYPE-046`. A copy of a `Vec`, one `Vec` assigned
over another, and a copy of a struct holding one are each refused where they
are written (`tests/probe/probe16f_vec_copy_refused.npk`, `probe16g`,
`probe16h`); against the library before, each compiled and read the
allocator's free poison through its second handle. The author's answer to the
workbench's question 9, 2026-09-25, and `nitpick-regex`'s design (its RX-161).
A transfer is `move(...)`, after which the source is refused
(`NITPICK-MOVE-001`); a `move Vec<T>:v` parameter takes ownership; and an
ordinary parameter is a LOAN, which for an owner is read-only at compiler
`c970483` — every write through it, `@v` included, is `NITPICK-TYPE-085` (the
compiler's DEF-102). So every function that changes a `Vec` takes `Vec<T>->`,
and `vec_at`, the one that only reads, takes a loan (S-18h).
`tests/unit/vec_moves.npk` runs every shape the property still allows. **It
costs nothing measured**: `#size_of<Vec<int64>>()` is 24 as before; no
module's arm bill moves; and the drop the compiler now generates for a `Vec`
walks the empty array and frees nothing, so the block is still `wild`,
`vec_free` is still its release, and a `Vec` never freed still traps
`WildLeak` at `exit 0` — S-18's guarantee is unchanged. `probe18` and
`probe18b` pin the language fact under the marker with no library code, and
redden first if a compiler changes it. **Not closed by it**: the four element
drops of S-18d, which no type here states.

**Rule S-18h (PD-45, cycle 0.1.3c) — the one verb that hands an element back
by value asks for a `T` that owns nothing, and the language decides which
types those are.** `vec_at<T: Pod>` reads through `Pod`'s `pod_copy`, a
`never fails` method whose `self` is LENT. For an owning type the body the
trait admits, `pass self`, is `NITPICK-TYPE-047` — a lent owner cannot be
passed on — so no owning type implements it as declared
(`tests/probe/probe19_pod_owner_refused.npk`), and `vec_at` at an owning `T`
is `NITPICK-TYPE-017` at the call (`generic_owning_copy/case5`, which until
this rule measured the read REMOVING the element). A type that owns nothing
implements `Pod` in one line where it is declared: the nine scalar impls —
the integers to 64 bits and `bool` — are in `src/core/vec.npk`, and the
umbrella re-exports the trait, so a consumer's struct of integers or a payload
enum does too (`tests/unit/vec_at_pod.npk`). The guard is S-17c's slice,
unchanged. **The rule's one hole is the compiler's**: an impl that declares
its `self` `move` where the trait lends it is accepted at compiler `c970483`
— the compiler's DEF-116, fixed at no pin of ours — and through `vec_at` it
hands back a second owner of the element: a double free, 95, measured. No
impl in this library does it, and none may; `TYPE-047`'s own message
suggests that `move`, and it is the one suggestion here not to take. **And the
trait is interim** (PD-49): the compiler's D-327, ratified on 2026-09-26 and in
no pin of ours, makes `Copy` a prelude marker trait over every copyable scalar,
derivable for a struct of copyables; at the re-pin that carries it, `Pod` is
replaced by `Copy` — its block in `src/core/vec.npk`, one bound, one call, one
umbrella line and three test impls — and this rule is restated for it.

**Rule S-19.** The generated zone tables are `fixed` module state — read-only
```

**Rule P-1b (PD-48, cycle 0.1.3c) — A′: an obligation is a comment unless a
numbered decision accepts the arm its live clause costs every consumer.** The
author's answer to `../OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` Q-6, 2026-09-25: *"the
recommendation on q-6 seems fine to me."* A `requires`, `ensures`,
`invariant` or `limit` is written LIVE only where a numbered decision says the
check earns the one `failsafe` identity it adds to every consuming program —
today that is TM-156's `ListLen` on the containers' lengths alone, which costs
every consumer of `core` `LimitViolated`. Every other obligation stays a
comment in the syntax it would take, `answer` and `outgoing` for `result` and
`old` (TM-130), is **evidence of nothing**, and is stood in for by a property
test — the sweeps of cycle 0.1, which check every day of the range, stand in
for `cal`'s. And:

- **`prove` stays a comment until the harness runs the verified build** (cycle
  0.8), because a plain build lowers it to nothing (`../OPEN_QUESTIONS.md`
  Q-6's table, measured at `c3bdae2`).
- **`decreases` and `unbounded` are the language's and always live** (D-304,
  P-9's note); `assert_static` is live wherever it helps, at no arm cost.
- **A check that guards something may be CODE on an arm every consumer already
  owes** — `weekday_index`'s `#unreachable()` belt (`SAFETY.md` S-15c) — which
  is not a contract and needs no decision.
- **Never a `requires` on an argument a caller supplies**: `SAFETY.md` S-12
  answers caller input with a `Result`, not a trap. **Nor on an accessor whose
  body already stops**: `vec_at`'s slice traps `OutOfBounds` (94) on an index
  out of range, and a live `requires` there would stop it first, as
  `RequiresViolated` (116), and add that arm to every consumer — measured at
  compiler `c970483`, cycle 0.1.3c's planning.

The switch for any row is mechanical — uncomment the clause, and record the
decision that accepts its arm — which is what P-1 promised and still delivers.
`nitpick-regex` records the same answer as its rule P-1b (its RX-164).

```
**Status, after cycle 0.1.3c: `Vec` is move-only by construction** — the
author's answer to the workbench's question 9, and `nitpick-regex`'s design,
ported. `Vec<T>`'s last field is `hidden string[0]:move_only` — zero bytes,
and its element owns — so a copy of a `Vec`, one assigned over another, or a
copy of anything that holds one is `NITPICK-TYPE-046` where it is written
(PD-44, `SAFETY.md` S-18g); against the library before, each compiled and read
freed memory through its second handle. **`vec_at` takes `T: Pod` and reads
through a loan** (PD-45, S-18h): `Pod`'s `pod_copy` lends its `self`, so an
owning type cannot implement it as declared (`NITPICK-TYPE-047`), and
`vec_at` at an owning `T` — which used to REMOVE the element — is refused at
the call, `NITPICK-TYPE-017`, which `generic_owning_copy/case5` asserts now.
The loan is the marker's consequence: at `c970483` the address of a lent owner
is `NITPICK-TYPE-085`, so a pointer reader could not be handed a `Vec` its
caller holds on loan. **`Bytes.body` is `hidden`** (PD-46): a consumer's write
through its pointer is `NITPICK-TYPE-080`, and the capacity is
`bytes_capacity`. **TM-150's churn pair is committed** with `heap:` bounds
(PD-47) — two million push-then-pop cycles at `T = string` peak at 120 bytes,
the same with `vec_clear` at all 48 000 096 — and **A′ is `VERIFICATION.md`
rule P-1b** (PD-48), which strikes Q-6. **`Pod` is interim** (PD-49): the
compiler's D-327, ratified during this cycle and in no pin of ours yet, makes
`Copy` a prelude marker trait, and the re-pin that carries it replaces `Pod`
with it — a confined edit, the trait's one block, one bound, one call, one
umbrella line, three test impls. The umbrella re-exports 59 names —
`Pod` and `bytes_capacity` joined — and eight files' managed memory is held to
the runtime's count <!-- {{sweep: heap_bounded=8}} -->. No arm bill moved. A

# Cycle 0.1.3c — `Vec` move-only by construction, ratified @DATE@

Six decisions, drafted at planning (`meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.3c.md` §2, PD-44 …
PD-49, in that order) and recorded here by the worker in the commit that makes
the change each describes. Every measurement is at compiler `c970483` and,
where it says so, at `c3bdae2`, taken at planning and re-derived at execution
by the plan's `facts.py`, `transcript.py` and `mut.py` — `0.1.3c.md` §1 and
§7, line for line. PD-46 was put to the orchestrator at planning as the
planner's judgement under the author's answer to question 9, and PD-49 was
taken at planning on the orchestrator's instruction, when the compiler's D-327
was ratified.

### TM-<PD-44> — `Vec<T>` is move-only by construction: its last field is a hidden zero-length array of an owning type, so a copy of a `Vec`, one `Vec` assigned over another, and a copy of a struct holding one are each refused `NITPICK-TYPE-046`

**@DATE@, cycle 0.1.3c (PD-44) — the author's answer to the workbench's
question 9 (2026-09-25 15:45), which left the shape to the port of
`nitpick-regex`'s design (its RX-161). Replaces TM-156 in part — its "What
it does NOT do", for `Vec` — and adds `SAFETY.md` S-18g.** `struct:Vec<T>`
ends `hidden string[0]:move_only`, and `vec_init` fills it `[]`. The compiler
answers whether an array owns by its element, whatever the length, and marks a
struct owning when any field is (its D-183); an owner is move-only.
**Measured at compiler `c970483`, and the same at `c3bdae2`:** with the marker
alone, a per-file sweep of the 116 tracked `.npk` moves no file;
`#size_of<Vec<int64>>()` is 24, as before; no module's arm bill moves — the
umbrella 13, `cal` 11, the rest 6; a `Vec` never freed still traps
`WildLeak`, 96, because the drop the compiler now generates for one walks the
empty array and frees nothing, so `vec_free` is still the block's release;
`Vec<int64>:w = v;`, `v = u;` and a copy of a struct holding a `Vec` are each
`NITPICK-TYPE-046`, once, at the copy (`probe16f`, `probe16g`, `probe16h`); and
`move(v)`, a struct built by moving a `Vec` in and then moved, a `move
Vec<int64>:v` parameter, and a lent `Vec` read through `vec_at` all run
(`tests/unit/vec_moves.npk`). **Two controls**: against the library before,
the three fixtures compile and read the allocator's free poison through their
second handle, exit 70 on both legs; and with the marker's element `int64` —
`hidden int64[0]:move_only`, which owns nothing — they compile again, so the
element's ownership is the mechanism and not the zero length. `probe18` and
`probe18b` pin that language fact with no library code.

*Alternatives declined* — `nitpick-regex`'s list (its `0.0.4d.md` §1.2
measured each at `c3bdae2`), re-read for this library: **a `string` marker
holding `""`** — the same verdicts and 24 bytes more on every `Vec`; it is the
fallback if a compiler refuses `T[0]`, which `probe18` would show first; **a
`buffer` marker** — 24 bytes, and a runtime call in every constructor; **a
`string?`** — 32 bytes, nothing bought; **an `OwnedFd`** — its drop is a
`close`, a syscall path under every `Vec` in a library whose one impure
module is `host` (TM-018); **the block itself in a managed `buffer`** — its
drop would free the block, `vec_free` would be redundant and D-151's gate would
stop covering a `Vec` (S-18): a redesign; **the prelude's `List<T>`** —
declined when the container question was answered on 2026-09-19 (keep our
`Vec`, give it the properties); **a language marker** — none exists at
`c970483`; **a harness check refusing a `Vec` copy in `src/`** — a house rule
where the compiler can refuse, blind to every consumer, which the author's
standing call on memory safety declines; **accept and document** — declined by
the author on question 9.

### TM-<PD-45> — `vec_at` takes `T: Pod` and reads through a loan: `Pod` is `nitpick-regex`'s trait, text for text, which an owning type cannot implement as it is declared, so `vec_at` at an owning `T` is refused `NITPICK-TYPE-017`, and `generic_owning_copy/case5` asserts that refusal

**@DATE@, cycle 0.1.3c (PD-45). Replaces TM-136 in part — its "`vec_at<T>`
is destructive" — adds `SAFETY.md` S-18h, and puts `Pod` on the umbrella.**
`pub trait:Pod = { func:pod_copy = Self(Self:self) never fails; };` and its
nine impls — `int8` to `int64`, `uint8` to `uint64` and `bool`, each `pass
self` of a LENT `self` — are `nitpick-regex`'s (its RX-168), the parameter
modes exact, declared in `src/core/vec.npk`. `vec_at` becomes `vec_at<T: Pod>
= T(Vec<T>:v, int64:i)`: the same slice over `count` (TM-129), and `pass raw
s[i].pod_copy();`. **Measured at compiler `c970483`, and the same at
`c3bdae2`:** the umbrella and every `src/` file compile, and no arm bill moves
— `pod_copy` is `never fails` and adds no identity; `impl:string:Pod`, an impl
for a struct holding a `string`, and `impl:Vec<int64>:Pod`, each written as
declared, are each `NITPICK-TYPE-047` (`probe19` asserts the first); a
consumer's struct of integers and a four-variant payload enum each implement
it in one line through the umbrella, and read back twice
(`tests/unit/vec_at_pod.npk`); and the read past `count` and the negative read
still trap `OutOfBounds`, 94. **`case5`** — the library's `vec_at` at `T =
string`, which exited 11 because the read REMOVED the element — is
`NITPICK-TYPE-017` at both of its calls, and asserts it: its marker moves, its
calls read the `Vec` as a loan, its record stays in its header, and
`TRANSCRIPT.txt` gains the three rows that say why — the text and the library
before, 11; this text against a loan `vec_at` WITHOUT the bound, 11; the
library, `NITPICK-TYPE-017`.

**Why a loan, where every other function here takes `Vec<T>->`:** the marker
makes a `Vec` an owner (TM-<PD-44>), and at `c970483` the address of a lent
owner is `NITPICK-TYPE-085` (the compiler's DEF-102). Measured: a function
handed a `Vec` by loan cannot call a pointer `vec_at` at all, nor can one handed
a struct that holds one, and `@` of another module's `sealed` `Vec` field is
`NITPICK-TYPE-079`. As a loan, `vec_at` reads all three, a local as `vec_at(v,
i)` and a pointer's pointee as `vec_at(<-p, i)`; and it is read-only by the
compiler's rule rather than by its own restraint. Every call in the tree moves
from `@v` to `v`: thirteen, in `vec_boundaries`, `vec_at_past_end` and `case5`.

**The one hole, stated.** An impl declaring `move string:self` where the trait
lends it is accepted at `c970483` — the compiler's DEF-116, raised by
`nitpick-regex` and fixed at no pin of ours — and through `vec_at` it hands
back a second owner of the element: read twice, one body freed twice, 95 on
both legs, measured. No impl here does it. This library adds no second pin of
the shape: `nitpick-regex`'s `probe18_impl_adds_move.npk` pins it, and the
compiler seat measured that pin as the whole of the ecosystem's exposure to
the fix.

*Alternatives declined:* **`vec_at` unchanged** — it compiles at `c970483`,
its slice being local where DEF-104's gate reads a lent or pointed-to
container, and at an owning `T` it goes on removing the element: the language
as specified, and the library saying so to no consumer; **`T: Pod` with
`Vec<T>->` kept** — the reads above, refused; **a loan WITHOUT the bound** —
measured, `case5` then exits 11: a move out of the caller's block through the
loan, the refusal routed around; **`.clone()` under the prelude's `Clone`** —
the prelude declares `clone` MAY FAIL, so in a `never fails` body it is
`Result<T>` (`NITPICK-TYPE-007`, and `NITPICK-TYPE-042` under `raw`, both
measured by `nitpick-regex`), an error channel on every read; **`struct:Vec<T:
Pod>`**, the four drops stated at the type for every verb — it refuses the
churn pair (TM-<PD-47>) and every owning measurement the restriction rests on,
and it waits on DEF-116, since an impl adding `move` would satisfy it;
`nitpick-regex` holds it open with a recommendation, and this library follows
that answer; **renaming `vec_at` to `vec_get`** — churn at every call site and
on the umbrella for a name, where the name is this library's since cycle 0.0.4
(TM-013); **`Pod` imported from `nitpick-regex`** — this library has no
dependencies (TM-027); **the name `Copy`** — a prelude may declare it, and
D-239 would then refuse ours; **a second pin of DEF-116's shape here** —
above.

### TM-<PD-46> — `Bytes.body` is `hidden`, and the capacity is read through `bytes_capacity`

**@DATE@, cycle 0.1.3c (PD-46) — the author's answer to question 9 let this
ride with `Vec`'s move-only property where the planner judged it cheaper, and
it is. Replaces TM-156 in part — `body` among the sealed fields, and its
declined "`body` hidden now" — and amends `SAFETY.md` S-17b and `BUILD.md`
B-12.** A sealed field admitted a write THROUGH its pointer (D-313): a
consumer's `b.body.ptr[0i64] = 65u8;` compiled and ran, `bytes_view` then read
65 where the sink had written 97, and a write one byte past the allocation ran
at exit 0. `hidden` refuses the member access itself (D-314): the same write is
`NITPICK-TYPE-080`, once (`probe16i`); against the library before, the fixture
compiles and reads the consumer's byte, exit 70. `pub func:bytes_capacity =
int64(Bytes->:b)` reads `b.body.cap`, and the umbrella re-exports it. **The
cost, measured:** sixteen code lines in three test files read `b.body.cap` —
`bytes_growth` 11, `bytes_view_lifetime` 4, `probe16e` 1 — and each reads
`bytes_capacity(@b)` now; nothing in `src/` outside `bytes.npk` read `body`;
the two heap-bounded `Bytes` tests measure what they did, 2 048 636 and 160;
and no arm bill moves. It takes the sink by pointer, as `bytes_len` does.

*Alternatives declined:* **`body` sealed, as TM-156 left it** — a consumer
corrupts a sink's bytes, or writes one past its allocation, with no
diagnostic: the argument that made `Vec.items` hidden; **its own subcycle after
the close** — the same sixteen lines and one accessor, later, and after cycle
0.4's formatters were written against the sealed field; `nitpick-regex` took
the same change in the subcycle of its marker (its RX-163); **a loan,
`bytes_capacity(Bytes:b)`** — every other `Bytes` reader takes `Bytes->`, and
whether they should read loans is cycle 0.4's to shape with a caller in hand
(TM-013).

### TM-<PD-47> — TM-150's churn pair is committed: `tests/unit/vec_churn_pop.npk` and `vec_churn_clear.npk`, two million push-then-pop cycles at `T = string` against the same with `vec_clear`, held to the runtime's count by TM-185's rule and under TM-186's cap

**@DATE@, cycle 0.1.3c (PD-47), carried here by `0.1.4b.md` §15.** TM-150
measured the pair at cycle 0.1.0b in a scratch directory — `peak_live` 120 for
the pop and 48 000 096 for the clear — and `SAFETY.md` S-18d,
`src/core/vec.npk` and the workbench's playbook quoted it with no program
behind it. **Derived from the source first**: a block of four 24-byte `string`
headers, 96 bytes, never grown; one 24-byte body a cycle; so the pop half peaks
at 96 + 24 = 120, the clear half at 96 + 2 000 000 × 24 = 48 000 096, and each
makes 2 000 001 allocations. **Measured at compiler `c970483` and at
`c3bdae2`, on both legs**: exactly those numbers, and `allocated=48000096` for
both halves. **The bounds, by TM-185's rule**: the leaking half `peak_live >=
48000000`, the leak's arithmetic; the remedy `count >= 2000000`, its work, and
`peak_live <= 75000` — the geometric mean of 120 and 48 000 096, 75 894,
rounded down to two significant figures. **The belt, by TM-186's**: both under
`cap: 65536 KiB`, the remedy exiting 0 and the leak 92, measured — `TESTING.md`
V-17 puts a cap on a pair with opposite outcomes under it, and this is one.
**Every bound was seen red on its mutant** (`0.1.3c.md` §7): the remedy with the
clear in the pop's place, by its ceiling and its cap; the leak with the pop in
the clear's place, by its floor and its cap; the remedy's loop emptied, by its
work floor, where a ceiling alone is fooled; and `vec_pop` without its `count`
decrement, by the remedy's exit and its ceiling.

*Alternatives declined:* **one program running both** — `peak_live` is a
whole-run high-water mark, so the clear's 48 MB would hide the pop's 120;
**in `tests/probe/`** — the pair asserts this library's own `vec_pop` and
`vec_clear`, which is `tests/unit/`'s business; the twin pairs in
`tests/probe/` carry their own look-alike `Vec`s; **no cap** — V-17's rule
admits one here, and then every twin pair carries the same two instruments.

### TM-<PD-48> — A′ replaces `VERIFICATION.md` P-1: an obligation is a comment unless a numbered decision accepts the arm its live clause costs every consumer; `prove` stays a comment until the verified build; Q-6 is struck

**@DATE@, cycle 0.1.3c (PD-48) — the author's answer to Q-6, 2026-09-25
15:56: *"the recommendation on q-6 seems fine to me."* Adds `VERIFICATION.md`
rule P-1b and marks P-1 superseded; restates `meta/roadmap/0.8/README.md`'s
P-1 item.** P-1's argument — every construct it names refuses, so a premature
clause is a build failure — has been false since compiler `c3bdae2` (Q-6's
table): `requires`, `ensures`, `invariant` and `limit` are live and each adds
one identity to every consuming program, and a plain build lowers `prove` to
nothing. **Rule P-1b**: a live contract only where a numbered decision accepts
its arm — today TM-156's `ListLen` alone; every other obligation a comment,
evidence of nothing, stood in for by a property test; `prove` a comment until
cycle 0.8's verified build; `decreases` and `unbounded` always live; a check
that guards something may be code on an arm consumers already owe (S-15c's
`#unreachable()`); and never a `requires` on caller input (S-12), nor on an
accessor whose body already stops. **Measured for that last clause at compiler
`c970483`, and the same at `c3bdae2`**: a live `requires i >= 0i64 && i <
v.count` on `vec_at` adds `RequiresViolated` to a consumer's bill — nine
identities to ten — and the read past the end stops at 116 where the slice
guard stops it at 94. Nothing in `src/` changes but the sentences that cited P-1
or Q-6 as open — two in `vec.npk`, three in `cal.npk`. `nitpick-regex`
records the same answer as its P-1b (its RX-164).

*Alternatives declined* — Q-6's own list: **A, live now** — an arm per
contract kind in every consumer, and a check per call until the verified build
elides it; measured at cycles 0.1.1 to 0.1.3, it adds no evidence the sweeps do
not already give; **B, every obligation a comment until 0.8, `assert_static`
included** — nothing checks a comment's syntax or its truth; **C, everything
live, `prove` included** — a plain build lowers `prove` to nothing, the silent
no-op P-1 was written against.

### TM-<PD-49> — `Pod` is ported now, at `c970483`, and the prelude's `Copy` replaces it mechanically at the re-pin that carries the compiler's D-327; nothing in this library is named `Copy`

**@DATE@, cycle 0.1.3c (PD-49) — taken at planning on the orchestrator's
instruction of 2026-09-26, when the author ratified the copy marker the
compiler seat had put to him, as the compiler's D-327: a PRELUDE marker trait
`Copy`, with impls for every copyable scalar in the generated scalar region, a
derivable form for a struct of copyables, and `List<T: Copy>`'s `list_get`
returning `T` `never fails`; and no `never fails` clone for an owning type. It lands at the compiler's 1.6.1c, after an advance
notice, and makes `Copy` a prelude-owned name (D-239); it is in no pin of
ours.** **The choice**: `vec_at<T: Pod>` now (TM-<PD-45>), shaped so that the
adoption of the pin carrying D-327 replaces it by edit and not by design —
`Pod` is one block of `src/core/vec.npk` (the trait and its nine impls), one
bound, one `pod_copy()` call, one umbrella line, and three impls in `tests/`
(`vec_at_pod`'s two and `probe19`'s), and it is used nowhere else. **At that
re-pin**, in this library and in `nitpick-regex`, whose `vec_get` has the same
shape: the block is deleted, the bound reads `T: Copy`, the call becomes the
copy D-327 spells, the umbrella's line goes, and the tests' impls take `Copy`'s
form — each measured then, since D-327's spellings are in no pin; and
`SAFETY.md` S-18h is restated for `Copy` by a decision that replaces this one
in part. **What it costs**: thirteen lines of trait and impls that a later
adoption deletes, and one public name taken away — which TM-013 allows while
the library is `0.x`, as it is.

*Alternatives declined:* **defer the bound until `Copy` lands** — `case5`'s
destructive read stays the library's public behaviour for three landings and a
re-pin, and `vec_at` has no good interim shape: after the marker (TM-<PD-44>) a
pointer reader cannot read a lent `Vec` (`meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.3c.md` §1.5),
and a loan without the bound moves an owning element out of the CALLER's block
(`case5` against the loan without the bound, 11) — so deferring the bound means
deferring the marker, and question 9's answer with it; **naming the trait `Copy` now**, so
that the replacement is a deletion — at that re-pin it collides with the
prelude-owned name and the tree is red for a reason that is not the re-pin's;
**holding 0.1.3c until D-327 lands** — the orchestrator's order is 0.1.3c and
then 0.1.5, and the cycle's close would wait on three landings and a re-pin;
**a `never fails` clone** — D-327 declines one for owning types, and the
prelude's `Clone` is fallible (TM-<PD-45>).
```

### 5.4 The numbers — `renumber.py`

<!-- script: renumber.py -->
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.3c (`0.1.3c.md` §7 step 4): the draft labels PD-44 ... PD-49 become the
next TM numbers, in order, and `@DATE@` becomes the work commit's date. In
`meta/DECISIONS.md` the new section's headings, the two supersession markers
and the cross-references are written `TM-<PD-44>` ...; each becomes its
number, and the `(PD-44)` provenance labels stay, as TM-184's `(PD-35)` does.
EVERYWHERE ELSE outside this plan, every `PD-44` ... `PD-49` becomes its
number. `@DATE@` is replaced in `DECISIONS.md` and in every file that carries
one of the six labels -- the struck question's heading carries it too -- AND
IN NO OTHER: earlier plans quote `@DATE@` in their hunks, and 0.1.4c's first
draft of this script, which replaced it everywhere, rewrote eleven dates in a
record at rehearsal. 0.1.4c's `renumber.py`, pointed at this plan's six labels,
reading and writing with `newline=""` so no byte it was not asked to change
moves.

    renumber.py FIRST_TM DATE

FIRST_TM is read by the plan's gate; DATE is the day of the work commit,
YYYY-MM-DD. Tracked AND untracked files -- `git grep` skips untracked files
unless told. Prints every file it changed and the counts; exit 1 if a label, a
placeholder or a date is left, or a heading is missing."""
import os, re, subprocess, sys

REPO = os.environ["REPO"]
first, date = int(sys.argv[1]), sys.argv[2]
if not re.fullmatch(r"\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}", date):
    sys.exit("STOP: DATE must be YYYY-MM-DD, not %r" % date)
pd0, count = 44, 6
MAP = {"PD-%d" % (pd0 + i): "TM-%d" % (first + i) for i in range(count)}
LABEL = re.compile(r"\b(?:%s)\b" % "|".join(re.escape(k) for k in MAP))
PATTERN = "|".join(r"PD-%d\b" % (pd0 + i) for i in range(count))
PLAN, DEC = "meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.3c.md", "meta/DECISIONS.md"


def read(path):
    with open(path, encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        return fh.read()


def write(path, text):
    with open(path, "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        fh.write(text)


def grep():
    out = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, "grep", "-l", "--untracked", "-E", PATTERN],
                         stdout=subprocess.PIPE, text=True).stdout.split()
    return [r for r in out if r != PLAN]


path = os.path.join(REPO, DEC)
dec = read(path)
placeholders = 0
for k, v in MAP.items():
    placeholders += dec.count("TM-<%s>" % k)
    dec = dec.replace("TM-<%s>" % k, v)
dates = dec.count("@DATE@")
dec = dec.replace("@DATE@", date)
write(path, dec)
print("  %-50s %d placeholder(s), %d date(s)" % (DEC, placeholders, dates))

changed, files = 0, [r for r in grep() if r != DEC]
for rel in files:
    p = os.path.join(REPO, rel)
    text = read(p)
    d = text.count("@DATE@")
    new, n = LABEL.subn(lambda m: MAP[m.group(0)], text.replace("@DATE@", date))
    write(p, new)
    changed += n
    print("  %-50s %d label(s), %d date(s)" % (rel, n, d))
left = [r for r in grep() if r != DEC] + [r for r in files if "@DATE@" in read(os.path.join(REPO, r))]
missing = [v for v in MAP.values() if not re.search(r"^### %s — " % v, dec, re.M)]
stale = "TM-<PD-" in dec or "@DATE@" in dec
print("labels renumbered: %d; PD-%d ... PD-%d -> TM numbers %d ... %d"
      % (changed, pd0, pd0 + count - 1, first, first + count - 1))
if missing:
    print("  STOP: meta/DECISIONS.md declares none of: %s" % ", ".join(missing))
if left:
    print("  STOP: labels or dates left in: %s" % ", ".join(left))
if stale:
    print("  STOP: a placeholder is left in meta/DECISIONS.md")
sys.exit(1 if missing or left or stale else 0)
```

---

## 6. The sweeps

Two, both by script, so the counts the record carries are the command's (the
workbench PLAYBOOK §12). §6.1 is the sweep every subcycle runs for what it
moves; §6.2 is the dispatch's REASON-SWEEP, which asks a different question —
not *"does a sentence state the old state?"* but *"does a live rule, check,
test or comment stand on a reason that is gone?"*.

### 6.1 The stale-claim sweep — a stale claim found once is a list

Run at step 5, after every group is applied and renumbered. **For each thing
this subcycle moves** — a `Vec` copyable, `vec_at` destructive and called
through a pointer, `Bytes.body` writable, P-1 in force and Q-6 open, the churn
pair without a program, and the totals — it greps for statements of the old
state over tracked and new files, the records excluded. **Every hit is read**,
and each must be one of three kinds: **edited** — changed by this subcycle and
now dated or true; **history** — a dated paragraph, a struck entry's record, a
ticked item, a cost note about an earlier cycle; or **still true** — about
something else. Any other is a stop.

<!-- script: stale.py -->
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.3c step 5: THE STALE-CLAIM SWEEP (`0.1.3c.md` §6.1), run by script so
the counts the record carries are the command's (the workbench PLAYBOOK §12).
For each thing this subcycle moves -- a `Vec` copyable, `vec_at` destructive
and taking a pointer, `Bytes.body` writable, P-1 in force and Q-6 open, the
churn pair without a program, the totals -- `git grep` for statements of its
OLD state over tracked AND new files, the RECORDS excluded: `meta/roadmap/done/`,
every earlier subcycle plan of cycle 0.1 and this one, the defect transcripts,
the tzdb spike's scratch, and `meta/DECISIONS.md`, whose settled text is never
rewritten. Every hit prints; §6.1's table says what each one is. A decision
this subcycle adds -- a number above TM-192, the highest at planning -- prints
as `TM-(new)`: the plan quotes this output, and `check_specs_current` reads a
TM number inside a fence as a citation."""
import os, re, subprocess

REPO = os.environ["REPO"]
EXCLUDE = [":!meta/roadmap/done/*", ":!meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.[0-4]*.md",
           ":!tests/probe/defect/*/TRANSCRIPT.txt", ":!meta/scratch/*",
           ":!meta/DECISIONS.md"]
PATTERNS = [
    ("S1", "a Vec copy stated as compiling",
     r"Vec<int64>:w = v|second handle|SECOND HANDLE|whole-struct copy|whole-`Vec` copy|"
     r"question 9, with|carries it to the author"),
    ("S2", "vec_at stated as a destructive read",
     r"REMOVES the element|removes the element|vec_at<T>` is destructive|DESTRUCTIVE|expect-exit: 11"),
    ("S3", "vec_at called through a pointer",
     r"vec_at(::<[a-z0-9]+>)?\(@"),
    ("S4", "Bytes.body stated as writable or read directly",
     r"body\.ptr\[i\] = x|still compiles and runs, unchecked|sealed and NOT hidden|sealed, not hidden|"
     r"[a-z]\.body\.cap\b"),
    ("S5", "P-1 in force, or Q-6 open",
     r"VERIFICATION\.md` P-1[^b]|Until Q-6 is answered|until Q-6|Q-6's recommended answer"),
    ("S6", "the churn pair without a program",
     r"push-then-pop|48 000 096|peak_live` 120"),
    ("S7", "the totals this subcycle moves",
     r"\b101 unit|\b116 (files|`\.npk`)|116 = |\b57 (public )?names|\b42 probe|"
     r"\b27 (files|positive)|[Ss]ix files|\b30 files|\b86 parse"),
]
total = 0
for tag, what, rx in PATTERNS:
    out = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, "grep", "-n", "-I", "--untracked", "-E", rx,
                          "--", "."] + EXCLUDE, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, text=True).stdout.splitlines()
    files = sorted({l.split(":", 1)[0] for l in out})
    total += len(out)
    print("%s %s: %d line(s) in %d file(s)" % (tag, what, len(out), len(files)))
    for l in sorted(out, key=lambda x: (x.split(":")[0], int(x.split(":")[1]))):
        f, n, text = l.split(":", 2)
        text = re.sub(r"\bTM-(\d+)", lambda m: "TM-(new)" if int(m.group(1)) > 192 else m.group(0), text)
        print("    %s:%s: %s" % (f, n, re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text.strip())[:110]))
print("stale-claim sweep: %d line(s) over %d pattern(s)" % (total, len(PATTERNS)))
```

### 6.2 The reason-sweep — what the six struck defects were cited FOR

**The dispatch's item 5.** The workbench registry struck six defects as
discharged at compiler `c970483`: O-N20 and O-N23, which this repository
raised (the compiler's DEF-99 and DEF-105), and four the other libraries
raised — the compiler's DEF-102, DEF-104, DEF-106 and DEF-108, the registry's
twenty-first, twenty-second, twenty-fourth and twenty-sixth entries.
**Striking a defect does not retire the rules it justified**: a
rule, a check, a test or a comment written BECAUSE of one stands on a reason
that is gone unless something re-reasoned it. This sweep finds every site that
cites one, and §6.2's table says what each cites it FOR and what it becomes.
Four of the six ids are not defined in this repository — only O-N20 and O-N23
were raised here — so this plan names the others by the compiler's number in
prose, and writes their ids only inside `reasons.py` and its output (the
workbench PLAYBOOK §6: a registry id a repository does not define is a
citation of an undefined question).

**Three passes**, because the id is not the only way a text cites a defect:
**I** the registry's six ids — the orchestrator's measurement at `3408c2b`,
reproduced exactly, 206 lines over 32 files; **D** the compiler's six numbers;
and **P** the two defects' SHAPES stated as current — *"compiles and faults"*,
*"does not stop the move"*, *"the importing module's scope"* — because a
sentence can state O-N20's hazard as live without naming it. **Pass P is what
found the one live rule still standing on a discharged defect**, `SAFETY.md`
§1's move-only row, which cites no id and which 0.1.4c's sweep — whose pattern
was `not stop a move` — missed by one word.

<!-- script: reasons.py -->
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.3c: THE REASON-SWEEP (`0.1.3c.md` §6.2) -- every line that cites one of
the six defects the workbench registry struck as discharged at compiler
`c970483`, by the registry's id, by the compiler's number, and by the shape of
the two whose weight is here; run at step 1 on the tree as it stands and again
at step 5. `git grep` over tracked AND new files, the plan itself excluded
(it quotes every id). Each hit prints with its file, line and a kind from the
file's place in the tree -- RECORD (a plan or execution record, a transcript,
`DECISIONS.md`'s settled text) or LIVE -- and §6.2's table says what each LIVE
site cites the defect FOR and what it becomes. The registry ids O-N21, O-N22,
O-N24 and O-N26 are not defined in this repository, so this script, and the
plan's quotation of its output, are the only places they are written (the
workbench PLAYBOOK §6: name the compiler's number in prose)."""
import os, re, subprocess

REPO = os.environ["REPO"]
PLAN = "meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.3c.md"
RECORD = re.compile(r"^meta/roadmap/(done/|0\.1/0\.1\.[0-4][a-c]?\.md$)|TRANSCRIPT\.txt$|^meta/DECISIONS\.md$")
PASSES = [
    ("I", "the registry's ids", r"\bO-N2[0-4]\b|\bO-N26\b", True),
    ("D", "the compiler's numbers", r"\bDEF-(99|102|104|105|106|108)\b", False),
    ("P", "the two shapes, stated as current",
     r"does not stop the move|not stop a (move|MOVE)|IMPORTING module's scope|"
     r"importing module's scope|compiles,? and (then )?faults", True),
]


def grep(rx):
    out = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, "grep", "-n", "-I", "--untracked", "-E", rx, "--", ".",
                          ":!" + PLAN], stdout=subprocess.PIPE, text=True).stdout.splitlines()
    return sorted(out, key=lambda l: (l.split(":", 2)[0], int(l.split(":", 2)[1])))


for tag, what, rx, detail in PASSES:
    hits = grep(rx)
    files = sorted({h.split(":", 1)[0] for h in hits})
    rec = [h for h in hits if RECORD.search(h.split(":", 1)[0])]
    print("%s %s: %d line(s) in %d file(s) -- %d RECORD, %d LIVE"
          % (tag, what, len(hits), len(files), len(rec), len(hits) - len(rec)))
    if tag == "I":
        for ident in ("O-N20", "O-N21", "O-N22", "O-N23", "O-N24", "O-N26"):
            h = grep(r"\b%s\b" % ident)
            print("    %-6s %3d line(s) in %2d file(s)" % (ident, len(h), len({x.split(":", 1)[0] for x in h})))
    per = {}
    for h in hits:
        f = h.split(":", 1)[0]
        per.setdefault(f, []).append(h)
    for f in sorted(per):
        kind = "RECORD" if RECORD.search(f) else "LIVE"
        lines = [h.split(":", 2)[1] for h in per[f]]
        if detail and kind == "LIVE":
            print("    %-6s %-58s %s" % (kind, f, ",".join(lines)))
        elif detail:
            print("    %-6s %-58s %d line(s)" % (kind, f, len(lines)))
        else:
            print("    %-6s %-58s %d line(s)" % (kind, f, len(lines)))
print("reason-sweep: done")
```

Run at step 1 on the unchanged tree — §7's `reasons` block is its whole output
— and again at step 5, where it must differ only by the lines §4 and §5 add
(`reasons_after`). **The measurement, and what every site is:**

| `tests/probe/defect/README.md` | 16, 17, 32, 40, 41 | the corpus table's struck rows and dated notes | history | stays |
| `tests/probe/defect/fixed_move_out/` | README 1, 35, 38; case 1, 2, 3 at line 3 | **O-N20's reproduction, its regression test** | current — a landed defect's reproduction is its regression test (TM-154, TM-189) | stays, for good |
| `tests/probe/defect/fixed_import_scope/` | README 1; `rows.npk` 5; case 1 at 3, 2 at 4, 5 at 9, 6 at 5, 7 at 5 | **O-N23's reproduction, its regression test** | current (TM-192) | stays, for good |
| `tests/probe/probe17_fixed_owning_reads.npk` | 19 | S-19b's positive twin: the move *"did not stop"* until `c970483` | current, dated | stays |
| `tests/unit/sweep/every_oracle_date.npk` | 39 | **the by-name import of `OracleYear`**, raised against O-N23 | current — **a belt since the fix** (TM-192) | stays |

**The rules the six justified, and what each became:**

- **The move out of `fixed` storage (O-N20, the compiler's DEF-99)** justified
  S-19b and `check_no_owning_fields`' widening, the hold on Z-4 and Z-6, and
  `fixed_move_out/`. 0.1.4c re-reasoned each: the rule and its check stand on
  the two refusals (TM-189) — a table whose rows own can be read neither by
  value (`TYPE-046`) nor by move (`TYPE-084`), and the check names such a table
  at its declaration, where the compiler would name its first read; the hold
  lifted (TM-191), and Z-6 reads by `.clone()` because `TYPE-084` refuses the
  obvious `pass` — the compiler's rule, not the defect; the reproduction is the
  regression test. **One sentence was missed, and no id could find it**:
  `SAFETY.md` §1's move-only row still says the language *"does not stop the
  move that faults"* — pass P's `SAFETY.md:22`. **This subcycle corrects it
  (§5.1)**, the one live rule text in the tree that still stood on a
  discharged defect.
- **The imported `fixed` binding's type (O-N23, DEF-105)** justified TM-181's
  by-name import and held cycle 0.5's tables. Both re-reasoned at 0.1.4c: the
  import stays, a belt that costs nothing (TM-192), and 0.5's "Watch for" keeps
  it as TM-181's convention; the reproduction is the regression test.
- **The loan (DEF-102)** has one citation by id, in a record. Its FIX is what
  this subcycle's loan rests on (PD-45, §1.5): pass D's new lines cite it as a
  current refusal, not as a defect.
- **The generic pass-out (DEF-104)** has no citation by id; `probe13d` names
  its refusal as current (TM-190), and the reason 0.1.4c gave for `vec_at`'s
  slice escaping the gate is carried into PD-45's declined *"`vec_at`
  unchanged"*.
- **The write into a part of `fixed` (DEF-106)** and **the function falling off
  its end (DEF-108)**: no citation here, and no rule of this repository's was
  written against either — 0.1.4c measured neither reaching the tree (its
  §1.6).

**Pass D, the compiler's numbers — 157 lines in 28 files at this plan's
commit**, 107 in records: every live one states a landing or a refusal as
current — each was written at 0.1.4c or later — or is a dated note. **Pass P,
the shapes — 61 lines in 13 files, 13 in live files**: twelve are a dated
paragraph, a struck entry, or a sentence its 0.1.4c note follows; **the
thirteenth is `SAFETY.md:22`**, above.

---

## 7. Steps, in order

Each step's expected output is the rehearsal's; the worker's own run is the
record, and **a difference is a finding, not a number to copy. Anything red
that a step does not predict is a stop.** Every block is compared by
`same.py`; a wall clock (`N.N s`) is set aside.

**Step 0 — the start, and the baseline.** Set this file's title to
`— RUNNING (since <date>, s2)` by hand — the only hand edit before step 8 —
then, after `source`-ing `env.sh` and §0's extraction:

```bash
( cd "$WB/.internal/toolchain/$PIN" && sha256sum -c SHA256SUMS )
git -C "$REPO" status --porcelain | wc -l
git -C "$REPO" rev-parse --short HEAD
grep -o '^### TM-[0-9]*' "$REPO/meta/DECISIONS.md" | tail -1
python3 --version
free -g | sed -n 2p
journalctl -u earlyoom --since today --no-pager | grep -c -i -E "sending sig|killing"
cd "$REPO" && python3 -B "$REPO/harness/run.py" > "$W/run0.log" 2>&1; echo "exit $?"
python3 -B "$CHECK/check_refs.py" "$REPO" | grep -v '^$'
```

Expected: two `OK`s; `1` — this file, just set `RUNNING` (the workbench
PLAYBOOK §12's *"N plus the plan file"*); this plan's commit; `### TM-192`;
`Python 3.12.3`; at least 32 GiB available (the board's floor for a dispatch);
`0` kills today — a higher count is the machine's until the journal's lines
say otherwise, so read them, and a kill of anything this subcycle started is a
stop (W-12); `exit 0`; and `check_refs` clean, `(80 md files via git
ls-files; leak scan 235 text of 235 tracked)`. Then the run's deciding
lines:

```bash
{ grep "FAIL\|exemption verdicts\|parse cleanly\|defect corpus\|^RED\|^GREEN" "$W/run0.log"; grep -o "[0-9]* unresolved, [0-9]* stale exemption(s)" "$W/run0.log"; grep -o "report: [A-Z]-[0-9]*[a-z]* cited" "$W/run0.log"; } | cut -c1-200 > "$W/step0.txt"
python3 -B "$W/same.py" step0 "$W/step0.txt"; echo "exit $?"
```

<!-- expect: step0 -->
```text
  ok    the verdict mechanisms                         3 specimen(s) for `_verdict`; exemption, defect corpus and header sweep each driven RED and silent on the control
      exemption verdicts: 5 of 5 re-derived, 0 moved
      defect corpus: 36 file(s) = 1 exempt (verdict re-derived above) + 35 asserted (20 run, 15 refusal, 0 unowned)
      86 parse cleanly, 28 parse and are refused by a later phase, 2 must not parse and do not, 0 failed
GREEN -- 101 unit(s), 0 failures; 5 pending, 125.8 s. Read this file's header for what that does not mean.
2 unresolved, 0 stale exemption(s)
report: S-18g cited
report: S-18h cited
```

**The two `report:` lines naming `S-18g` and `S-18h` are expected**: this
plan cites the two rules its work commit declares, and `check_specs_current`
reports an unresolved citation rather than failing on one; after step 4 they
are gone.

**Step 1 — every fact, re-derived before anything changes** (§1), and the
reason-sweep on the tree as it stands (§6.2). About 90 s; it builds nothing in
the tree, and it saves step 6's baseline sweep.

<!-- script: facts.py -->
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.3c step 1: EVERY FACT THIS PLAN RESTS ON, re-derived BEFORE anything is
changed (`0.1.3c.md` §1 names each). HEAD's tracked tree is copied with `git
archive` to $W/facts/before/; the plan's own `code` group is applied to a
second copy, $W/facts/port/, by the plan's own `apply.py`; and every other
variant is one named substitution on one of those two -- so what is measured is
the planned text and not a paraphrase of it. Programs are written under each
variant's `m/x/` and built there. Nothing in the tree is written; step 6's
baseline, $W/sweep_<PIN>.txt, is saved from part B. Sources are read and
written as BYTES (the workbench PLAYBOOK §6). One line per fact; the worker
compares the whole output with §7's `facts` block by `same.py`.

  A  the pin: both digests at both pins, and the anchor unchanged
  B  every tracked `.npk` at the pin, saved for step 6
  C  the variants: `vec.npk` and the umbrella compiled in each, at both pins
  D  the marker ALONE over every tracked `.npk`: what it moves
  E  the programs: each claim of §1, at both pins where it says so, both legs
  F  the arm bills a consumer of `vec`, `bytes` and the umbrella owes, before
     and after -- `NITPICK-REACH-002`'s own list"""
import hashlib, io, os, re, shutil, subprocess, sys, tarfile

REPO, WB, W = os.environ["REPO"], os.environ["WB"], os.environ["W"]
PIN, OLD = os.environ["PIN"], os.environ["OLD"]
PINS = (OLD, PIN)
OUT = os.path.join(W, "facts")
if os.path.isdir(OUT):
    shutil.rmtree(OUT)
os.makedirs(OUT)
DIAG = re.compile(rb"^(NITPICK-[A-Z]+-\d+) (\S+?):(\d+):(\d+):", re.M)
HEAP = re.compile(rb"heap: allocated=(\d+) peak_live=(\d+) count=(\d+)")
ASKS = re.compile(rb"does not name `([^`]+)`")


def tool(pin, name):
    return os.path.join(WB, ".internal", "toolchain", pin, name)


def rd(path):
    with open(path, "rb") as fh:
        return fh.read()


def wr(path, data):
    os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(path), exist_ok=True)
    with open(path, "wb") as fh:
        fh.write(data if isinstance(data, bytes) else data.encode("utf-8"))


def codes(out, root):
    seen, got = set(), []
    for m in DIAG.finditer(out):
        code, path, line, col = (x.decode() for x in m.groups())
        if code not in seen:
            seen.add(code)
            where = "" if os.path.basename(path) == root else os.path.basename(path) + ":"
            got.append("%s@%s%s:%s" % (code, where, line, col))
    return got


def npkc(pin, src, ll="/dev/null"):
    d, b = os.path.split(src)
    p = subprocess.run([tool(pin, "npkc"), b, "-o", ll], cwd=d,
                       stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT)
    return p.returncode, codes(p.stdout, b), p.stdout


def build_run(pin, src, leg, heap=False, cap=None):
    """npkc -> [opt -O2] -> llc -> ld.lld -> run, and the first step that fails
    names the verdict; a run's verdict is its exit (128+N for a signal)."""
    stem = os.path.basename(src)[:-4]
    d = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(src), "%s.%s" % (pin, leg))
    os.makedirs(d, exist_ok=True)
    ll, obj, exe = (os.path.join(d, stem + x) for x in (".ll", ".o", ""))
    st, got, _ = npkc(pin, src, ll)
    if st != 0 or not os.path.isfile(ll):
        return "npkc:" + ",".join(got), ""
    if leg == "O2":
        if subprocess.run(["opt", "-O2", "-S", ll, "-o", ll + ".opt"]).returncode:
            return "opt", ""
        ll, flag = ll + ".opt", "-O2"
    else:
        flag = "-O0"
    if subprocess.run(["llc", flag, "-filetype=obj", "-relocation-model=static",
                       ll, "-o", obj]).returncode:
        return "llc", ""
    if subprocess.run(["ld.lld", "-static", obj, tool(pin, "npkrt.o"), "-o", exe]).returncode:
        return "ld", ""
    env = ["env", "-i", "NTIME_HARNESS=1"] + (["NPK_HEAP_STATS=1"] if heap else [])
    pre = None
    if cap:
        import resource

        def pre():
            resource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_AS, (cap * 1024, cap * 1024))
    r = subprocess.run(env + [exe], stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
                       preexec_fn=pre)
    h = HEAP.findall(r.stderr)
    note = " ".join("allocated=%s peak_live=%s count=%s" % tuple(x.decode() for x in t) for t in h)
    return "run:%d" % (r.returncode if r.returncode >= 0 else 128 - r.returncode), note


def legs(pin, src, heap=False, cap=None):
    v0, h0 = build_run(pin, src, "O0", heap, cap)
    if v0.startswith("npkc"):
        return v0
    v2, h2 = build_run(pin, src, "O2", heap, cap)
    out = "%s %s" % (v0, v2)
    if heap:
        out += "  " + (h0 if h0 == h2 else "O0 %s | O2 %s" % (h0, h2))
    return out


def sub(root, rel, old, new):
    """One substitution in a variant; it must match EXACTLY ONCE, or the fact
    it serves would be measured on a variant nobody planned (PLAYBOOK §9)."""
    p = os.path.join(root, rel)
    t = rd(p)
    n = t.count(old.encode())
    if n != 1:
        sys.exit("STOP: %s: the substitution's old text occurs %d times, not once:\n%s"
                 % (rel, n, old[:200]))
    wr(p, t.replace(old.encode(), new.encode()))


def variant(name, base, subs=()):
    root = os.path.join(OUT, name)
    shutil.copytree(os.path.join(OUT, base), root)
    for rel, old, new in subs:
        sub(root, rel, old, new)
    return root


FS = """func:failsafe = int32(Error:e) {
    pick (e) {
        (HeapBadRequest)    { exit 91i32; },
        (HeapOom)           { exit 92i32; },
        (IntOverflow)       { exit 93i32; },
        (OutOfBounds)       { exit 94i32; },
        (Unreachable)       { exit 95i32; },
        (WildLeak)          { exit 96i32; },
        (DivByZero)         { exit 97i32; },
        (DivOverflow)       { exit 98i32; },
        (StackExhausted)    { exit 106i32; },
        (MachineFault)      { exit 107i32; },
        (DecreasesViolated) { exit 108i32; },
        (LimitViolated)     { exit 109i32; },
        (RequiresViolated)  { exit 116i32; },
        (*)                 { exit 99i32; }
    }
    exit 9i32;
};
"""
CATCH_ALL = """func:failsafe = int32(Error:e) {
    pick (e) {
        (*)                 { exit 99i32; }
    }
    exit 9i32;
};
"""
USE_VEC = 'use "../../src/core/vec.npk".*;\n'


def program(root, stem, body, fs=FS, extra=()):
    d = os.path.join(root, "m", "x")
    for name, text in extra:
        wr(os.path.join(d, name), text)
    path = os.path.join(d, stem + ".npk")
    wr(path, "mod:%s;\n%s\n%s" % (stem, body, fs))
    return path


def row(label, what, var, pin, verdict):
    print("  %-3s %-34s %-13s %-8s %s" % (label, what, var, pin, verdict))


# ---- A. the pin ---------------------------------------------------------------
print("A. the pin")
for pin in PINS:
    sums = {}
    for line in rd(tool(pin, "SHA256SUMS")).decode().splitlines():
        h, name = line.split()
        sums[name] = h
    for name in ("npkc", "npkrt.o"):
        data = rd(tool(pin, name))
        h = hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()
        print("  %-8s %-8s %9d B  %s  %s" % (pin, name, len(data), h,
              "== SHA256SUMS" if sums.get(name) == h else "!= SHA256SUMS"))

# ---- B. every tracked .npk at the pin, for step 6 ---------------------------------
print("B. every tracked .npk at %s, rooted at npkc from its own directory" % PIN)
files = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, "ls-files", "*.npk"], stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                       text=True, check=True).stdout.split()
base = {}
for rel in files:
    st, got, _ = npkc(PIN, os.path.join(REPO, rel))
    base[rel] = "%-62s exit %d %s" % (rel, st, " ".join(got))
wr(os.path.join(W, "sweep_%s.txt" % PIN), "\n".join(base[r] for r in files) + "\n")
ok = sum(1 for r in files if base[r].split(" exit ")[1].startswith("0"))
print("  %d file(s): %d compile, %d refused; saved as sweep_%s.txt for step 6"
      % (len(files), ok, len(files) - ok, PIN))

# ---- C. the variants ----------------------------------------------------------------
print("C. the variants -- src/core/vec.npk and src/lib.npk, each compiled alone")
arch = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, "archive", "HEAD"], stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                      check=True).stdout
before = os.path.join(OUT, "before")
tarfile.open(fileobj=io.BytesIO(arch)).extractall(before)
port = variant("port", "before")
env = dict(os.environ, REPO=port, PLAN=os.path.join(REPO, "meta/roadmap/0.1/0.1.3c.md"))
p = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-B", os.path.join(W, "apply.py"), "code"], env=env,
                   stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT, text=True)
if p.returncode:
    sys.exit("STOP: the plan's code group did not apply to the copy:\n" + p.stdout)
print("  port: %s" % p.stdout.strip().splitlines()[-1])
VEC = "src/core/vec.npk"
STRUCT = "hidden string[0]:move_only; };"
LOAN_AT = "pub func:vec_at<T: Pod> = T(Vec<T>:v, int64:i) never fails {"
BODY_AT = "    pass raw s[i].pod_copy();\n};"
marker = variant("marker", "before", [
    (VEC, "sealed limit<ListLen> int64:cap; };",
     "sealed limit<ListLen> int64:cap; hidden string[0]:move_only; };"),
    (VEC, "count: 0i64, cap: n };", "count: 0i64, cap: n, move_only: [] };")])
marker_int64 = variant("marker_int64", "port", [(VEC, STRUCT, "hidden int64[0]:move_only; };")])
podptr = variant("podptr", "port", [(VEC, LOAN_AT, LOAN_AT.replace("Vec<T>:v", "Vec<T>->:v"))])
loan_nobound = variant("loan_nobound", "port", [
    (VEC, LOAN_AT, LOAN_AT.replace("<T: Pod>", "<T>")),
    (VEC, BODY_AT, "    pass s[i];\n};")])
port_req = variant("port_req", "port", [
    (VEC, LOAN_AT, LOAN_AT.replace("never fails {", "never fails requires i >= 0i64 && i < v.count {"))])
clone_raw = variant("clone_raw", "port", [
    (VEC, LOAN_AT, LOAN_AT.replace("<T: Pod>", "<T: Clone>")),
    (VEC, BODY_AT, "    pass raw s[i].clone();\n};")])
clone_plain = variant("clone_plain", "port", [
    (VEC, LOAN_AT, LOAN_AT.replace("<T: Pod>", "<T: Clone>")),
    (VEC, BODY_AT, "    pass s[i].clone();\n};")])
VARIANTS = [("before", before), ("marker", marker), ("port", port), ("marker_int64", marker_int64),
            ("podptr", podptr), ("loan_nobound", loan_nobound), ("port_req", port_req),
            ("clone_raw", clone_raw), ("clone_plain", clone_plain)]
for name, root in VARIANTS:
    got = []
    for pin in PINS:
        for rel in (VEC, "src/lib.npk"):
            st, cs, _ = npkc(pin, os.path.join(root, rel))
            got.append("%s %s %s" % (pin, os.path.basename(rel)[:-4],
                                     "0" if st == 0 else ",".join(c.split("@")[0] for c in cs)))
    print("  %-13s %s" % (name, "  ".join(got)))

# ---- D. the marker alone, over every tracked .npk ------------------------------------
print("D. the marker alone -- every tracked .npk of HEAD, against `marker`'s src/, at %s" % PIN)
moved = []
for rel in files:
    st, got, _ = npkc(PIN, os.path.join(marker, rel))
    line = "%-62s exit %d %s" % (rel, st, " ".join(got))
    if line != base[rel]:
        moved.append((rel, base[rel], line))
print("  %d file(s) swept, %d differ from HEAD's" % (len(files), len(moved)))
for rel, a, b in moved:
    print("    %s\n      HEAD   %s\n      marker %s" % (rel, a.split(" exit ", 1)[1], b.split(" exit ", 1)[1]))

# ---- E. the programs ---------------------------------------------------------------
print("E. the programs -- label, what, variant, pin, then -O0 and opt -O2")
for name in ("probe16f_vec_copy_refused", "probe16g_vec_assign_refused",
             "probe16h_vec_holder_copy_refused", "probe16i_bytes_body_write_refused"):
    rel = "tests/probe/%s.npk" % name
    for root in (before, marker_int64):
        wr(os.path.join(root, rel), rd(os.path.join(port, rel)))
SIZE = USE_VEC + 'use "../../src/core/bytes.npk".*;\n' + """
func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    if (#size_of<Vec<int64>>() != 24i64)  { exit 10i32; }
    if (#size_of<Vec<string>>() != 24i64) { exit 11i32; }
    if (#size_of<Bytes>() != 32i64)       { exit 12i32; }
    exit 0i32;
};
"""
for name, root in (("before", before), ("port", port)):
    for pin in PINS:
        row("E1", "sizes: Vec 24, Vec<string> 24, Bytes 32", name, pin, legs(pin, program(root, "sizes", SIZE)))
for label, name in (("E2", "probe16f_vec_copy_refused"), ("E3", "probe16g_vec_assign_refused"),
                    ("E4", "probe16h_vec_holder_copy_refused")):
    for vname, root in (("before", before), ("marker_int64", marker_int64), ("port", port)):
        for pin in PINS:
            row(label, name, vname, pin, legs(pin, os.path.join(root, "tests/probe/%s.npk" % name)))
for vname, root in (("before", before), ("port", port)):
    for pin in PINS:
        row("E5", "probe16i_bytes_body_write_refused", vname, pin,
            legs(pin, os.path.join(root, "tests/probe/probe16i_bytes_body_write_refused.npk")))
LEAK = USE_VEC + """
func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    Vec<int64>:v = raw vec_init::<int64>(4i64);
    drop vec_push(@v, 7i64);
    exit 0i32;
};
"""
for vname, root in (("before", before), ("port", port)):
    row("E6", "a Vec never freed", vname, PIN, legs(PIN, program(root, "never_freed", LEAK)))
HOLDER = """mod:holder;
use "../../src/core/vec.npk".*;
pub struct:Box = { sealed Vec<int64>:items; int64:tag; };
pub func:box_make = Box() never fails {
    Vec<int64>:v = raw vec_init::<int64>(4i64);
    drop vec_push(@v, 7i64);
    pass Box{ items: move(v), tag: 1i64 };
};
pub func:box_free = NIL(Box->:b) never fails {
    drop vec_free(@b.items);
    pass NIL;
};
"""
READS = {
    "lent_param": ("a lent Vec, read", """
func:first = int64(Vec<int64>:v) never fails {
    pass raw vec_at::<int64>(%s, 0i64);
};
func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    Vec<int64>:v = raw vec_init::<int64>(4i64);
    drop vec_push(@v, 7i64);
    int64:x = raw first(v);
    drop vec_free(@v);
    if (x != 7i64) { exit 10i32; }
    exit 0i32;
};
""", ("@v", "v")),
    "lent_field": ("a lent struct's Vec field, read", """
struct:Holder = { Vec<int64>:v; int64:tag; };
func:first = int64(Holder:h) never fails {
    pass raw vec_at::<int64>(%s, 0i64);
};
func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    Vec<int64>:a = raw vec_init::<int64>(4i64);
    drop vec_push(@a, 7i64);
    Holder:h = Holder{ v: move(a), tag: 1i64 };
    int64:x = raw first(h);
    drop vec_free(@h.v);
    if (x != 7i64) { exit 10i32; }
    exit 0i32;
};
""", ("@h.v", "h.v")),
    "sealed_field": ("another module's sealed Vec, read", """use "./holder.npk".*;
func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    Box:b = raw box_make();
    int64:x = raw vec_at::<int64>(%s, 0i64);
    drop box_free(@b);
    if (x != 7i64) { exit 10i32; }
    exit 0i32;
};
""", ("@b.items", "b.items")),
    "pointee": ("a pointer's pointee, read", """
func:first = int64(Vec<int64>->:p) never fails {
    pass raw vec_at::<int64>(%s, 0i64);
};
func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    Vec<int64>:v = raw vec_init::<int64>(4i64);
    drop vec_push(@v, 7i64);
    int64:x = raw first(@v);
    drop vec_free(@v);
    if (x != 7i64) { exit 10i32; }
    exit 0i32;
};
""", ("p", "<-p")),
}
for stem, (what, body, (ptr, loan)) in READS.items():
    for vname, root, arg in (("podptr", podptr, ptr), ("port", port, loan)):
        extra = (("holder.npk", HOLDER),) if stem == "sealed_field" else ()
        row("E7", "%s, `%s`" % (what, arg), vname, PIN,
            legs(PIN, program(root, stem, USE_VEC + (body % arg), extra=extra)))
case5 = "tests/probe/defect/generic_owning_copy/case5_vec_at_destructive.npk"
for vname, root in (("before", before), ("loan_nobound", loan_nobound), ("port", port)):
    for pin in PINS:
        row("E8", "case5, as that variant's tree has it", vname, pin, legs(pin, os.path.join(root, case5)))
OWNERS = {
    "impl_string": "impl:string:Pod = { func:pod_copy = string(string:self) never fails { pass self; }; };\n",
    "impl_named": ("struct:Named = { string:name; int64:n; };\n"
                   "impl:Named:Pod = { func:pod_copy = Named(Named:self) never fails { pass self; }; };\n"),
    "impl_vec": ("impl:Vec<int64>:Pod = { func:pod_copy = Vec<int64>(Vec<int64>:self) never fails "
                 "{ pass self; }; };\n"),
}
for stem, decl in OWNERS.items():
    row("E9", "Pod written as declared: %s" % stem, "port", PIN,
        legs(PIN, program(port, stem, USE_VEC + decl + "func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {\n    exit 0i32;\n};\n")))
HOLE = USE_VEC + """impl:string:Pod = { func:pod_copy = string(move string:self) never fails { pass self; }; };
func:twice = int64(Vec<string>->:v) never fails {
    string:a = raw vec_at::<string>(<-v, 0i64);
    string:b = raw vec_at::<string>(<-v, 0i64);
    pass string_byte_length(a) + string_byte_length(b);
};
func:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {
    Vec<string>:v = raw vec_init::<string>(2i64);
    drop vec_push(@v, string_concat("Europe/", "Kyiv_zzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzz"));
    int64:n = raw twice(@v);
    drop vec_free(@v);
    if (n != 82i64) { exit 10i32; }
    exit 0i32;
};
"""
for pin in PINS:
    row("E10", "Pod with `move string:self`, read twice", "port", pin, legs(pin, program(port, "hole", HOLE)))
PAST = USE_VEC + """
func:main = int32(cstring[]:argv) {
    Vec<int64>:v = raw vec_init::<int64>(8i64);
    drop vec_push(@v, 10i64);
    drop vec_push(@v, 20i64);
    if (argv.len < 1i64) { exit 10i32; }
    discard(raw vec_at(v, v.count));
    drop vec_free(@v);
    exit 11i32;
};
"""
for vname, root in (("port", port), ("port_req", port_req)):
    for pin in PINS:
        row("E11", "vec_at past count", vname, pin, legs(pin, program(root, "past_end", PAST)))
for vname, root in (("port", port), ("port_req", port_req)):
    path = program(root, "past_end_bill", PAST, fs=CATCH_ALL)
    st, _, out = npkc(PIN, path)
    asks = sorted(set(x.decode() for x in ASKS.findall(out)))
    row("E12", "its consumer's bill", vname, PIN, "%d: %s" % (len(asks), " ".join(asks)))
COUNT080 = []
for rel in ("tests/unit/bytes_growth.npk", "tests/unit/bytes_view_lifetime.npk",
            "tests/probe/probe16e_sealed_reads.npk"):
    root = os.path.join(OUT, "old_tests_new_src")
    if not os.path.isdir(root):
        shutil.copytree(os.path.join(port, "src"), os.path.join(root, "src"))
    wr(os.path.join(root, rel), rd(os.path.join(before, rel)))
    st, _, out = npkc(PIN, os.path.join(root, rel))
    n = len(re.findall(rb"^NITPICK-TYPE-080 ", out, re.M))
    COUNT080.append("%s %d" % (os.path.basename(rel)[:-4], n))
row("E15", "the Bytes tests as they stood, port's src", "port", PIN,
    "TYPE-080 at " + ", ".join(COUNT080))
for stem in ("vec_churn_pop", "vec_churn_clear"):
    for pin in PINS:
        row("E13", stem, "port", pin, legs(pin, os.path.join(port, "tests/unit/%s.npk" % stem), heap=True))
    row("E14", stem + " under 65536 KiB", "port", PIN,
        legs(PIN, os.path.join(port, "tests/unit/%s.npk" % stem), cap=65536))

# ---- F. the arm bills ----------------------------------------------------------------
print("F. the arm bills -- what NITPICK-REACH-002 asks of a consumer importing one module")
for rel in ("src/core/vec.npk", "src/core/bytes.npk", "src/lib.npk"):
    got = {}
    for vname, root in (("before", before), ("port", port)):
        path = program(root, "bill_" + os.path.basename(rel)[:-4],
                       'use "../../%s".*;\nfunc:main = int32(cstring[]:_~argv) {\n    exit 0i32;\n};\n' % rel,
                       fs=CATCH_ALL)
        st, _, out = npkc(PIN, path)
        got[vname] = sorted(set(x.decode() for x in ASKS.findall(out)))
    print("  %-20s before %2d, port %2d: %s" % (rel, len(got["before"]), len(got["port"]),
          "the same" if got["before"] == got["port"] else "DIFFER: %s | %s" % (got["before"], got["port"])))
print("facts: done")
```

lines §4 and §5 insert above it; pass D gains FOUR, each a current statement of
a landed refusal — PD-45's two (DEF-102, DEF-104), S-18g's and `vec.npk`'s
