<!-- script: mut.py -->
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.3c step 6: EVERY NEW ASSERTION SHOWN RED ON A WRONG WORLD, judged by the
harness's own `stages.refusal` and `stages.program` -- the code that judges the
real tree -- in a copy of the APPLIED tree's `src/` and `tests/` under
$W/mut/<row>/ (0.1.4b's `mut.py`, pointed at this subcycle's files). Every
substitution is asserted to match EXACTLY ONCE, so a row whose mutation matched
nothing cannot read as a caught mutant (`PLAYBOOK.md` §9), and every red row is
read by its NAMED LINES, never by a status alone. Then the declined
formulation, shown FOOLED where the chosen one is not, and last the churn
pair's ceiling recomputed from the measured numbers by TM-185's rule. Prints
`differences from the plan: N`; exit 0 only at 0."""
import math, os, shutil, subprocess, sys

REPO, W = os.environ["REPO"], os.environ["W"]
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(REPO, "harness"))
import build as build_mod, manifest as manifest_mod, stages   # noqa: E402

MUT = os.path.join(W, "mut")
MAN = manifest_mod.load(REPO)
VEC, BYTES = "src/core/vec.npk", "src/core/bytes.npk"
F16F, F16G, F16H = ("tests/probe/probe16f_vec_copy_refused.npk",
                    "tests/probe/probe16g_vec_assign_refused.npk",
                    "tests/probe/probe16h_vec_holder_copy_refused.npk")
F16I = "tests/probe/probe16i_bytes_body_write_refused.npk"
P19 = "tests/probe/probe19_pod_owner_refused.npk"
CASE5 = "tests/probe/defect/generic_owning_copy/case5_vec_at_destructive.npk"
POP, CLEAR = "tests/unit/vec_churn_pop.npk", "tests/unit/vec_churn_clear.npk"
POD = "tests/unit/vec_at_pod.npk"
diffs = 0


def tree(name, subs=(), head=()):
    """A copy of the applied `src/` and `tests/`; `head` names files taken from
    HEAD -- this plan's commit -- instead, which is the library BEFORE."""
    root = os.path.join(MUT, name)
    if os.path.isdir(root):
        shutil.rmtree(root)
    for d in ("src", "tests"):
        shutil.copytree(os.path.join(REPO, d), os.path.join(root, d))
    for rel in head:
        data = subprocess.run(["git", "-C", REPO, "show", "HEAD:" + rel], stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
                              check=True).stdout
        with open(os.path.join(root, rel), "wb") as fh:
            fh.write(data)
    for rel, old, new in subs:
        p = os.path.join(root, rel)
        t = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read()
        if t.count(old) != 1:
            sys.exit("STOP: row %s: in %s the old text occurs %d times, not once"
                     % (name, rel, t.count(old)))
        open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(t.replace(old, new))
    return root


def judge(root, rel, markers=None):
    bld = build_mod.Build(root, MAN, os.environ["NPKC"], os.environ["NPKRT"],
                          os.path.join(root, "build"))
    if markers is not None:
        p = os.path.join(root, rel)
        t = open(p, encoding="utf-8").read().splitlines(True)
        keep = [l for l in t if not l.startswith(("// heap:", "// cap:"))]
        open(p, "w", encoding="utf-8").write(keep[0] + "".join("// %s\n" % m for m in markers)
                                             + "".join(keep[1:]))
    e = stages.read(root, rel)
    return (stages.refusal(bld, rel, e) if e.is_refusal else stages.program(bld, rel, e)), e


def row(name, rel, problems, e, want):
    """`want`: None for green, else the texts the problems must contain."""
    global diffs
    text = " | ".join(" ".join(p.split()) for p in problems)
    peak = " ".join("%s=%s" % (f, e.measured.get("-O0", {}).get(f, "-"))
                    for f in ("peak_live", "count")) if e.heap else ""
    got = "green" if not problems else "red"
    ok = (want is None and not problems) or (
        want is not None and problems and all(w in text for w in want))
    if not ok:
        diffs += 1
    print("%-4s %-40s %-5s %-30s %s" % (name, os.path.basename(rel), got, peak,
                                        "MATCH" if ok else "DIFFER"))
    for w in (want or []):
        print("       named: %s -- %s" % (w, "found" if w in text else "MISSING"))
    if not ok:
        print("       said:  %s" % (text or "nothing"))


REFUSED = "npkc exited 0. The header expects it to refuse with %s."
# ---- M0: the applied tree, unchanged ---------------------------------------------
root = tree("M0")
MEASURED = {}
for rel in (F16F, F16G, F16H, F16I, P19, CASE5, POD, POP, CLEAR):
    pr, e = judge(root, rel)
    if e.heap:
        MEASURED[rel] = e.measured.get("-O0", {}).get("peak_live")
    row("M0", rel, pr, e, None)

# ---- A: the library before -- HEAD's src/, the applied tests ----------------------
root = tree("A", head=(VEC, BYTES, "src/lib.npk"))
for rel, code in ((F16F, "NITPICK-TYPE-046"), (F16G, "NITPICK-TYPE-046"),
                  (F16H, "NITPICK-TYPE-046"), (F16I, "NITPICK-TYPE-080")):
    pr, e = judge(root, rel)
    row("A", rel, pr, e, [REFUSED % code])

# ---- B: the marker over an element that owns nothing ----------------------------
root = tree("B", [(VEC, "hidden string[0]:move_only; };", "hidden int64[0]:move_only; };")])
for rel in (F16F, F16G, F16H):
    pr, e = judge(root, rel)
    row("B", rel, pr, e, [REFUSED % "NITPICK-TYPE-046"])

# ---- C: vec_at's loan WITHOUT its bound; Pod's hole; body sealed again -----------
LOAN_AT = "pub func:vec_at<T: Pod> = T(Vec<T>:v, int64:i) never fails {"
root = tree("C1", [(VEC, LOAN_AT, LOAN_AT.replace("<T: Pod>", "<T>")),
                   (VEC, "    pass raw s[i].pod_copy();\n};", "    pass s[i];\n};")])
pr, e = judge(root, CASE5)
row("C1", CASE5, pr, e, [REFUSED % "NITPICK-TYPE-017"])
root = tree("C2", [(P19, "string(string:self)", "string(move string:self)")])
pr, e = judge(root, P19)
row("C2", P19, pr, e, [REFUSED % "NITPICK-TYPE-047"])
root = tree("C3", [(BYTES, "pub struct:Bytes = { hidden buffer:body;",
                    "pub struct:Bytes = { sealed buffer:body;")])
pr, e = judge(root, F16I)
row("C3", F16I, pr, e, [REFUSED % "NITPICK-TYPE-080"])

# ---- P: a scalar impl missing ------------------------------------------------------
root = tree("P1", [(VEC, "impl:uint32:Pod = { func:pod_copy = uint32(uint32:self) never fails "
                         "{ pass self; }; };\n", "")])
pr, e = judge(root, POD)
row("P1", POD, pr, e, ["npkc failed", "NITPICK-TYPE-017"])

# ---- K: the churn pair, each wrong the way its bound exists for -------------------
POPLINE = ("        string:s = raw vec_pop(@v);\n"
           "        if (string_byte_length(s) != 24i64) { exit 10i32; }\n")
root = tree("K1", [(POP, POPLINE, "        drop vec_clear(@v);\n")])
pr, e = judge(root, POP)
K1 = e.measured["-O0"]["peak_live"]
row("K1", POP, pr, e, ["peak_live is 48000096; the header bounds it <= 75000",
                       "exited 92; the header expects exit 0"])
root = tree("K2", [(CLEAR, "        drop vec_clear(@v);\n", POPLINE)])
pr, e = judge(root, CLEAR)
row("K2", CLEAR, pr, e, ["peak_live is 120; the header bounds it >= 48000000",
                         "exited 0; the header expects exit 92"])
LOOP = "    while (i < 2000000i64) decreases 2000000i64 - i {\n"
EMPTY = "    while (i < 0i64) decreases 2000000i64 - i {\n"
root = tree("K3", [(POP, LOOP, EMPTY)])
pr, e = judge(root, POP)
row("K3", POP, pr, e, ["count is 1; the header bounds it >= 2000000"])
K3_ROOT = root
root = tree("K4", [(VEC, "    T:answer = move(s[v.count - 1i64]);\n    v.count = v.count - 1i64;\n",
                    "    T:answer = move(s[v.count - 1i64]);\n")])
pr, e = judge(root, POP)
row("K4", POP, pr, e, ["exited 11; the header expects 0"])

# ---- THE DECLINED FORMULATION, FOOLED where the chosen one is not -----------------
print("declined formulation -- must be FOOLED:")
pr, e = judge(K3_ROOT, POP, ["heap: peak_live <= 75000", "cap: 65536 KiB, exit 0"])
row("D1", POP, pr, e, None)        # a ceiling alone, on the emptied remedy (K3)

# ---- THE CEILING, RECOMPUTED BY TM-185's RULE -------------------------------------
g = math.sqrt(MEASURED[POP] * K1)
k = 10 ** (len(str(int(g))) - 2)
rule = int(g) // k * k
good = rule == 75000 and MEASURED[POP] <= rule < K1
if not good:
    diffs += 1
print("ceiling -- the geometric mean of the correct peak and the mutant's, rounded down to two significant figures:")
print("  %-34s correct %9d  mutant %9d  rule %9d  header %9d  %s"
      % (os.path.basename(POP), MEASURED[POP], K1, rule, 75000, "SAME" if good else "DIFFER"))
print("differences from the plan: %d" % diffs)
sys.exit(0 if diffs == 0 else 1)
```

<!-- script: sweep.py -->
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.3c step 6: every `.npk` in the tree AFTER the port -- the tracked
ones and the new ones not yet added -- rooted at $NPKC from its own directory,
in `facts.py`'s format, saved to $W/sweep_after.txt and compared, file by
file, with step 1's $W/sweep_<PIN>.txt: each file that is new or whose line
changed is printed with both lines, then the counts and the codes."""
import os, re, subprocess

REPO, W, PIN = os.environ["REPO"], os.environ["W"], os.environ["PIN"]
DIAG = re.compile(rb"^(NITPICK-[A-Z]+-\d+) (\S+?):(\d+):(\d+):", re.M)
files = sorted(set(subprocess.run(
    ["git", "-C", REPO, "ls-files", "--cached", "--others", "--exclude-standard", "*.npk"],
    stdout=subprocess.PIPE, text=True, check=True).stdout.split()))


def line(rel):
    d, b = os.path.split(os.path.join(REPO, rel))
    p = subprocess.run([os.environ["NPKC"], b, "-o", "/dev/null"], cwd=d,
                       stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT)
    seen, got = set(), []
    for m in DIAG.finditer(p.stdout):
        code, path, ln, col = (x.decode() for x in m.groups())
        if code not in seen:
            seen.add(code)
            where = "" if os.path.basename(path) == b else os.path.basename(path) + ":"
            got.append("%s@%s%s:%s" % (code, where, ln, col))
    return "%-62s exit %d %s" % (rel, p.returncode, " ".join(got))


after = {rel: line(rel) for rel in files}
with open(os.path.join(W, "sweep_after.txt"), "w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
    fh.write("\n".join(after[r] for r in files) + "\n")
before = {}
with open(os.path.join(W, "sweep_%s.txt" % PIN), encoding="utf-8") as fh:
    for l in fh.read().splitlines():
        before[l.split()[0]] = l
new = [r for r in files if r not in before]
gone = [r for r in before if r not in after]
changed = [r for r in files if r in before and before[r].rstrip() != after[r].rstrip()]
print("%d file(s) after, %d before: %d new, %d gone, %d changed, %d the same"
      % (len(after), len(before), len(new), len(gone), len(changed),
         len(after) - len(new) - len(changed)))
def verdict(l):
    """What a line says after its path -- `exit N` and the codes."""
    return "exit " + l.split(" exit ", 1)[1].rstrip()


for r in changed:
    print("  changed %s\n      before %s\n      after  %s" % (r, verdict(before[r]), verdict(after[r])))
for r in new:
    print("  new     %s\n      after  %s" % (r, verdict(after[r])))
for r in gone:
    print("  GONE    %s" % r)
ok = sum(1 for r in files if " exit 0 " in after[r] + " ")
tally = {}
for r in files:
    for c in re.findall(r"NITPICK-[A-Z]+-\d+", after[r]):
        tally[c] = tally.get(c, 0) + 1
print("after: %d compile, %d refused; codes: %s" % (ok, len(files) - ok, "  ".join(
    "%s x%d" % (c[len("NITPICK-"):], n) for c, n in sorted(tally.items()))))
```

**Step 8 — CI, and the record.**

<!-- script: ci_read.py -->
```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""0.1.3c step 8: CI's job log, read by script rather than by eye.
`gh run view --log` can print nothing for a run whose log exists (the
workbench PLAYBOOK §6), so the log is fetched through the jobs API into a
file, and this prints the lines that decide the run -- each GitHub timestamp
removed, and CI's own `npkc` digest, which is reported and never asserted,
reduced to whether it is there -- for step 8 to compare with its block.

    ci_read.py LOGFILE"""
import re, sys

with open(sys.argv[1], "rb") as fh:
    text = fh.read().decode("utf-8", "replace")
lines = [re.sub(r"^\S+Z ", "", l).rstrip() for l in text.splitlines()]
WANT = (r"^compiler HEAD == ", r"^llvm-config --version == ", r"^npkc   WB: ",
        r"^npkrt  CI: ", r"^npkrt  WB: ", r"^built from compiler ",
        r"exemption verdicts: ", r"defect corpus: ", r"parse cleanly, ",
        r"tests/unit/vec_churn_", r"case5_vec_at_destructive",
        r"^GREEN -- ", r"^RED -- ")
for l in lines:
    if any(re.search(rx, l) for rx in WANT):
        print(l.strip())
ci = [l for l in lines if l.startswith("npkc   CI: ")]
print("npkc   CI: %s" % ("reported, one line" if len(ci) == 1 else "%d line(s)" % len(ci)))
print("unqualified GREEN lines: %d" % sum(1 for l in lines if l.startswith("GREEN -- ")))
```

```bash
gh run list -R alternative-intelligence-cp/nitpick-time -L 1
gh run watch <id> -R alternative-intelligence-cp/nitpick-time --exit-status; echo "exit $?"
gh api repos/alternative-intelligence-cp/nitpick-time/actions/runs/<id>/jobs --jq '.jobs[0].id'
gh api repos/alternative-intelligence-cp/nitpick-time/actions/jobs/<job>/logs > "$W/ci.log"; echo "exit $?"
python3 -B "$W/ci_read.py" "$W/ci.log" > "$W/ci.txt"; echo "exit $?"
python3 -B "$W/same.py" ci "$W/ci.txt"; echo "exit $?"
```

## 8. What will bite

- **`earlyoom` is live** (the board's hazard 12; SIGTERM 143 before SIGKILL
  137). A step that dies by signal may be the machine: read
  `journalctl -u earlyoom --since today` **before** diagnosing anything, and
  treat a kill as a stop, never a retry. One full harness at a time, and
  `free -g` first.
- **`TYPE-047`'s message gives the wrong advice for `Pod`.** Refused for a
  lent owner's `pass self`, it suggests declaring the parameter `move` — and at
  `c970483` an impl that does is ACCEPTED (the compiler's DEF-116, not in this
  pin) and makes `vec_at` a double free (§1.6). Never write a `Pod` impl with a
  `move` anywhere; `probe19` is refused, and stays refused, as written.
- **`vec_at` takes the `Vec`, not its address.** Every call is `vec_at(v, i)`
  for a local or a lent `Vec`, `vec_at(s.f, i)` for a field, and
  `vec_at(<-p, i)` through a pointer. Written `@v` it is refused, and at a
  generic call with `@v` refused the compiler adds `NITPICK-TYPE-022` — the
  cascade its DEF-117 fixes at a later landing; a turbofish removes it.
- **`case5` is refused TWICE, once per call** — two `TYPE-017` diagnostics,
  one code. B-7 holds the SET of codes, so that is one expectation met; do not
  "tidy" it to one call.
- **`apply.py` stops at the first block that does not apply**, having written
  nothing for it — but the blocks before it ARE applied. After a stop, read
  `git -C "$REPO" status --short`, find the cause, `git -C "$REPO" checkout --`
  the group's files and `rm` its new ones through `"${REPO:?}"`, and re-run the
  group; never hand-apply the rest. `apply.py check GROUP` answers without
  writing.
- **`transcript.py` and `renumber.py` run once.** The first refuses its own
  section; the second finds nothing left. A hand edit between two runs is not
  re-checked.
- **A TM number in this file is a citation** — `check_refs` counts inline code,
  and `check_specs_current` reads fences too. Write *"PD-44, the number after
  TM-192"*, never the six new numbers; and never write *"supersedes"* before a
  settled decision's number on one line, which `check_refs` reads as a
  supersession to be marked — the texts here say *"replaces … in part"*.
- **A registry id this repository does not define is a citation of an
  undefined question** in any tracked markdown file's prose. Name the
  compiler's DEF number instead (the workbench PLAYBOOK §6); §6.2 writes the
  four such ids only inside its script and its output.
- **The marker block of a `.npk` header is contiguous from line 1** (TM-121):
  every new header puts its markers on lines 1 … N, and no prose line below
  begins `// <marker>:` — `case5`'s record quotes its old marker as
  `expect-exit: 11` in running prose, never at a line's start.
- **A literal past `int64`'s range is `NITPICK-LEX-004`** even at `uint64`:
  `vec_at_pod` reads `~0u64`, TM-149's spelling of the maximum.
- **`grep` here is ugrep**: given several files it prints in completion order,
  so every step greps one file at a time.
- **No absolute path goes into this file's record**: `check_refs`' leak scan
  reads every tracked text file, and the scripts print relative names only.
- **Every removal names its target through `"${VAR:?}"`**.

## 11. What 0.1.3c hands on, and in what order

**The order is 0.1.3c, then 0.1.5** — one writer in one repository, so serial.

- **0.1.5 — the close, the ecosystem's third, which triggers the ecosystem-wide
  audit (W-22).** It owes its README row's list — the public `README.md`'s
  status, `nitpick.toml`'s `check` item, `core.npk`'s placeholder count,
  `check_error_budget`'s message, the probe table's rows for probes 12 to 16e
  (this subcycle wrote rows for the seven it added, and none for the rest),
  the `tools/` line, the sweep stage's headroom, `.gitignore`'s fixtures line,
  and `run_defect_corpus`' *"six subdirectories"* — and 0.1.4c's suggestion on
  the harness's text-mode reads of source. **From this subcycle**, three
  things the audit should read: (1) **the reason-sweep's method** (§6.2): a
  struck defect is found in a live rule by its shape as well as its id — the
  close's own sweep should run pass P over whatever it restates; (2) **the
  five new files that run** — `probe18`, the churn pair and the two positive
  twins — each name exactly the arms `NITPICK-REACH-002` asks, this cycle's
  newest measurement of that list; and (3) **nothing here
  moves `CALENDAR.md` or the cycle's gate** — the close certifies the calendar,
  and this subcycle changed `core`.
- **The next re-pin** — decided when the compiler's notice 71 lands: at 71,
  or once after 73 (the board, 2026-09-26). It carries notices 67 (DEF-107's
  view freeze, `NITPICK-BORROW-015`, and DEF-109 to DEF-115), 69 (DEF-116 and
  DEF-117) and 71 (1.6.1 step 1: every emission gains a first `target
  datalayout` line, and every library manifest two required `[toolchain]`
  rows, `triple` and `datalayout` — the board's advance notice F15); taken
  after 73, it carries D-327 as well, and the next bullet's replacement is the
  same adoption's. **What of this plan moves there — stated in advance, and
  MEASURED ahead of it at planning** against the workbench's control copy of
  the compiler at `9f6f370` (notices 67, 69 and 70 — not the pin, and without
  71's emission line; §12 has the commands):
  - **No verdict moves.** A per-file sweep of the tree at this plan's commit
    (116 files) and of the ported tree (127) gives every file, at `9f6f370`,
    the exit, the codes and the positions it gets at `c970483`, and
    the full harness run over the ported tree is `GREEN -- 112 unit(s), 0
    failures; 5 pending`, its 194 lines identical to the run at `c970483`
    with the wall clocks set aside — every exit, every refusal, every heap
    number and both caps.
  - **DEF-116's fix refuses a `move`-adding impl** (`NITPICK-TYPE-014`),
    measured: §1.6's E10 — the hole, 95 at `c970483` — is refused
    `NITPICK-TYPE-014` at its `move` parameter, and `mut.py`'s row C2, the same
    `move` put into `probe19`'s impl, is refused `TYPE-014` too, so C2's named
    line changes (the mutant stays red). The sentences that say DEF-116 is fixed
    *"at no pin of ours"* — `vec.npk`'s `Pod` comment, `SAFETY.md` S-18h,
    `probe19`'s header, PD-45's text — become history for the adoption to
    date. **No committed test moves**: every `Pod` impl here matches the
    trait, and `probe19` is still `TYPE-047`, at the same position.
  - **DEF-117 removes the `TYPE-022` cascade**: no committed file asserts
    `TYPE-085`, so nothing here moves; §1.5's programs already use the
    turbofish.
  - **DEF-107's freeze**: the compiler seat measured zero exposure over the
    231 files it swept, and the eleven files this subcycle adds and the seven

- **For the workbench**, three things, none a compiler defect. (1) **The

  element out of the caller's storage at `c970483` (`case5` against

  not raised as a defect here — the compiler seat may judge whether a move out
  of a `#wild_slice` over a loan should be refused like DEF-102's writes.
  (3) **For the reason-sweep's own discipline**: the registry's strike and
  this repository's rules are two lists; 0.1.4c's sweep caught 206 lines by id

## 12. How this plan was rehearsed — which command ran where

**Four passes in fresh clones beside a linked workbench, then one in the REAL
checkout, `$REPO` itself, on this plan's commit** — each run as the worker
runs it: every fenced `bash` block of §0 and §7 extracted from this file by
index and run verbatim in a fresh `bash`, with only `REPO=` (block 0) or the
`source` line prepended; this file's title set `RUNNING` before step 0's run,
the one hand edit; every step's output saved and compared with its block by
`same.py`. Step 8's block, which holds `<id>`, never ran. **The clones** were
taken from the real checkout at `3408c2b`, each beside a scratch workbench
whose `BOARD.md`, `WORKSTREAMS.md`, `PLAYBOOK.md`, `README.md`, `skills/` and
`.internal/toolchain/` are links to the real ones — so `env.sh`'s `$WB`,
`$CHECK` and both toolchains resolved as they do in the real position, and
nothing was written in the workbench — and each clone carried this plan's
commit as a scratch commit of the same three files, so `HEAD` was the plan's
commit, as the gate asks. **The measurements before the passes** — the
unchanged tree at both pins, the per-file sweeps with and without the marker,
the variants, §1's programs and the arm bills — were taken in the planner's
scratch copies, never in the tree, and `facts.py` re-derived every one of them
in every pass.

| 1 | clone, 08:19–08:25 | before PD-49 was written in | every block ran to its end; its outputs became the first expected blocks; finding 3 below |
| 2 | clone, 08:30–08:37 | PD-49 in | every block ran; findings 1 and 2 below |

| 4 | clone, 08:55–09:02 | this file's blocks | **every compared block `0 difference(s)`** — `step0` 8 lines, `facts` 86, `reasons` 83, `step2` 100, `tx` 25, `step4` 38, `stale` 91, `reasons_after` 84, `final` 31, `mut` 47, `sweep` 30, `step7` 42 |

**Found by the passes and corrected above** — each a defect of the plan's
text, none of the work it plans:

1. **The compiler's suggestion number for the copy marker was read as a

**The next re-pin, measured ahead of it** (§11). The workbench keeps a
CONTROL copy of the compiler at `9f6f370` (`$WB/.internal/toolchain/9f6f370/`,
its `PIN.md`: notices 67, 69 and 70 — not the pin, and without 1.6.1 step 1's

116 files at this plan's commit and of the 127 after the port, each compared

`harness/run.py` over the ported tree with `NPKC` and `NPKRT` pointed at it —

**Every leg ran, in the real position.** `facts.py`, `transcript.py` and
`mut.py` find the kept toolchains through `$WB`, which `env.sh` resolves from
`$REPO`, so nothing that reads a checkout-relative path could have been
skipped (the workbench PLAYBOOK §12). **Not rehearsed:** the work and record
commits, the push and CI — §7's `ci` block is a prediction from the
workflow's own `echo` lines, 0.1.4c's CI log at the same pin and step 6's
local run; and `renumber.py` with a first number other than 193.

**Then restored**, after the real pass: `git -C "$REPO" reset -q --hard` to
this plan's commit, `$W` removed through `"${W:?}"`, and `build/` put back
byte for byte from a copy taken before the pass; afterwards `git status
--porcelain` was empty, `HEAD` the plan's commit, no stash, and `$W` absent —
so the worker starts from nothing of the rehearsal's. **One ignored directory
could not be put back, and need not be**: the harness's own. Its self-check re-runs
`run.py` through `sys.executable` WITHOUT `-B` (`harness/selfcheck.py`), so
`-B` on the command line does not reach that child, and it rewrote
`harness/__pycache__/checks.cpython-312.pyc` and `stages.cpython-312.pyc` at
step 6, compiled from the ported sources; and every run regenerates the
self-check's scratch under `.internal/scratch/selfcheck/` — the same files at
the same sizes, new times. Both are gitignored and neither can be put back:
the reset rewrote `checks.py` and `stages.py` with new times, so the old
bytecode would be stale as well, and the worker's first run writes both
afresh in any case. The clones and the
planner's measurements stayed in its session scratch, outside every
repository.

**`earlyoom` during the planning**: its journal, read before the first
